.

4.2
Cell atlas querying

We demonstrate the performance of Algorithm
2
in constructing
prediction sets for query cells’ joint latent representations with a hold-out experiment.

First, we randomly held out one
CD8 effector
cell, one
double negative T
cell, and one
pre-B
cell as test data and performed the same procedure as in the previous subsection on the remaining
6320
cells to obtain the OrchAMP cell atlas (Figure
2
top left).
Next,
we queried the atlas with the observation within each single modality (i.e.

ATAC, RNA, and Protein
)

respectively

for each held-out cell, constructed the
95\%
prediction set of the joint latent representation of the query cell using Algorithm
2
.

Finally, we visualized each prediction set by first sampling
500
points in the set uniformly at random and then mapping
them
to the constructed atlas using the
MapQuery
function in the Seurat V4
R
package.

For illustration, we visualized the prediction sets for the held-out
double negative T
cell when queried only with its ATAC, RNA, or Protein observations, respectively, in the top right, the bottom left, and the bottom right panels of Figure
2
, respectively.

Analogous plots for the other two held-out cells are deferred to the Section
A
as the main message remains similar.
With reference to cell type locations in the atlas in the top left panel, all three prediction sets were contained predominantly within the correct cell population.
Moreover, the prediction sets typically occupied only a subset of the full cell-type cluster, thereby conveying information beyond a coarse cell-type label.

5
Theoretical results

In this section, we study the asymptotic properties of the OrchAMP estimators constructed by Algorithm
1
, as well as the asymptotic coverage probabilities of the prediction sets obtained from Algorithm
2
.

Our theoretical analysis of Algorithm
1

builds upon and further develops the framework
in
[
59
]
:
Assumption 5.1 below
generalizes Assumption 5.1 of
[
59
]
,
Proposition 5.1 extends Proposition 5.2 therein,
and Theorem 5.1 generalizes
the conclusions of their Theorem 5.4 to multi-modal settings.
While the proof strategies are closely connected,
adapting them to the multi-modal
setting requires substantial work.
Specifically,
Zhong et al., [59]
analyzed a single AMP orbit associated with a single data matrix, whereas our orchestrated framework involves multiple parallel AMP orbits, each corresponding to a different high-dimensional modality whose leading singular vectors are to be recovered.
Therefore, the analysis must account for synchronous information integration at each iterate across these orbits via
respective denoisers, a signature in our analysis that was absent in the single-orbit setting.

Beyond estimation, we contribute new theoretical results on the asymptotic validity of the prediction sets constructed by Algorithm
2
.
Establishing these results requires novel arguments based on empirical process techniques, which is orthogonal to the scope of
[
59
]
and forms a distinct component of our theory.

5.1
Assumptions

We begin with the following assumptions on the model (
1.1
)–(
1.2
).

Assumption 5.1
.

The matrices
\{\bm{U}_{h},\bm{D}_{h},\bm{V}_{h},\bm{W}_{h}:h\in[m]\}
,
\{\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{\ell},\widetilde{\bm{W}}_{\ell}:\ell\in[\widetilde{m}]\}
and the prior classes
\mathcal{P}
and
\{\mathcal{P}_{\nu_{h}}:h\in[m]\}
satisfy the following assumptions:

1.

All
\mu\in\mathcal{P}
are absolutely continuous with respect to the Lebesgue measure and have bounded density.

2.

For all
\mu\in\mathcal{P}
, we have
\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[U_{h}U^{\top}_{k}]<\infty
,
\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[U_{h}\widetilde{U}^{\top}_{\ell}]<\infty
and
\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[\widetilde{U}_{\ell}\widetilde{U}^{\top}_{g}]<\infty
, where
h,k\in[m]
;
\ell,g\in[\widetilde{m}]
and
(U_{1},\ldots,U_{m},\widetilde{U}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{U}_{\widetilde{m}})\sim\mu
. Furthermore,
\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[U_{h}U^{\top}_{h}]=\bm{I}_{r_{h}}
and
\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[\widetilde{U}_{\ell}\widetilde{U}^{\top}_{\ell}]=\bm{I}_{\widetilde{r}_{\ell}}
for all
h\in[m]
and
\ell\in[\widetilde{m}]
.

3.

For all
h\in[m]
,
\nu_{h}\in\mathcal{P}_{\nu_{h}}
satisfies
\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{h}}[V_{h}V^{\top}_{h}]=\bm{I}_{r_{h}}
, where
V_{h}\sim\nu_{h}
.

4.

The collection of the matrices
\{\bm{U}_{1},\ldots,\bm{U}_{m},\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{\widetilde{m}}\}
is independent of the collection of the matrices
\{\bm{V}_{1},\ldots,\bm{V}_{m}\}
.

5.

For all
h\in[m]
and
k\in[r_{h}]
, the signal strengths
(\bm{D}_{h})_{kk}>\gamma^{-1/4}_{h}
.

6.

For any collection of positive semi-definite matrices
\{\bm{A}_{h},\bm{B}_{h}\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{h}\times r_{h}}:h\in[m]\}
, and
\{\widetilde{\bm{A}}_{\ell}\in\mathbb{R}^{{\widetilde{r}}_{\ell}\times{\widetilde{r}}_{\ell}}:\ell\in[\widetilde{m}]\}
and
\mu\in\mathcal{P}
, there exists an open neighborhood
O_{\mu}
of
\mu
in the topology of weak convergence, such that for

Y^{G}_{h}\sim N_{r_{h}}(\bm{A}_{h}U_{h},\bm{B}_{h}),\quad\widetilde{Y}^{G}_{\ell}\sim N_{{\widetilde{r}}_{\ell}}(\widetilde{\bm{A}}_{\ell}\widetilde{U}_{\ell},\bm{I}_{{\widetilde{r}}_{\ell}}),\quad\mbox{and}\quad(U_{1},\ldots,U_{m},\widetilde{U}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{U}_{\widetilde{m}})\sim\mu,

the functions
u^{G}_{h}:\mathbb{R}^{r+\widetilde{r}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{r_{h}}
and
\widetilde{u}^{G}_{\ell}:\mathbb{R}^{r+\widetilde{r}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{\widetilde{r}_{\ell}}
, given by

\displaystyle u^{G}_{h}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m},\widetilde{x}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{x}_{\widetilde{m}};\mu)

(5.1)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[U_{h}|Y^{G}_{1}=x_{1},\ldots,Y^{G}_{m}=x_{m},\widetilde{Y}^{G}_{1}=\widetilde{x}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{Y}^{G}_{\widetilde{m}}=\widetilde{x}_{\widetilde{m}}],\quad\text{and}

(5.2)

\displaystyle\widetilde{u}^{G}_{\ell}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m},\widetilde{x}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{x}_{\widetilde{m}};\mu)

(5.3)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[\tilde{U}_{\ell}|Y^{G}_{1}=x_{1},\ldots,Y^{G}_{m}=x_{m},\widetilde{Y}^{G}_{1}=\widetilde{x}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{Y}^{G}_{\widetilde{m}}=\widetilde{x}_{\widetilde{m}}],

(5.4)

respectively, are uniformly Lipschitz over the neighborhood
O_{\mu}
for all
h\in[m]
and
\ell\in[\widetilde{m}]
.

7.

For any positive semi-definite matrices
\bm{A}^{R}_{h},\bm{B}^{R}_{h}\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{h}\times r_{h}}
, and
\nu_{h}\in\mathcal{P}_{\nu_{h}}
,
h\in[m]
, there exists an open neighborhood
O_{\nu_{h}}
of
\nu_{h}
in the topology of weak convergence, such that for

Y^{G}_{h,R}\sim N_{r_{h}}(\bm{A}^{R}_{h}V_{h},\bm{B}^{R}_{h}),\quad\mbox{where}\quad V_{h}\sim\nu_{h},

the function
v^{G}_{h}:\mathbb{R}^{r_{h}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{r_{h}}
given by

\displaystyle v^{G}_{h}(x;\nu_{h})=\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{h}}[V_{h}|Y^{G}_{h,R}=x],

(5.5)

is uniformly Lipschitz over the neighborhood
O_{\nu_{h}}
, for all
h\in[m]
.

The first assumption on
\mu
is a regularity condition that is only required for
establishing the asymptotic coverage of the constructed prediction sets. It is valid for a wide range of distribution classes
\mathcal{P}
including Gaussian mixture models.
The second and the third assumptions guarantee identifiability of the latent factors
\bm{U}_{h}
,
\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{\ell}
, and the factor loading matrices
\bm{V}_{h}
for
h\in[m]
and
\ell\in[\widetilde{m}]
.
The fourth assumption can be generalized slightly to allow dependence among the
\bm{V}_{h}
’s, while we state it in its current form to avoid estimating higher-dimensional priors without clear benefits.

Part 5 of Assumption
5.1

is an eigengap assumption which lands all high-dimensional modality in the super-critical regime
[
6
, Theorem 2.8]
and ensures for all
h\in[m]
that the leading
r_{h}
sample singular values of
\bm{X}_{h}
are separated from the bulk
and so
the leading
r_{h}
singular vectors of
\bm{X}_{h}
have non-zero correlations with the true signals.
The latter property is critical to the successful initialization of Algorithm
1
with sample SVD.
While seemingly strong, it
holds for most motivating data examples in single-cell biology.
In the rest of this section,
we assume oracle knowledge of the matrices
\bm{D}_{1},\ldots,\bm{D}_{m}
for notational convenience
6
6

6

Under Assumption
5.1
part 5,
the matrices
\{\bm{D}_{h}:h\in[m]\}
are consistently estimated by the matrices
\{\widehat{\bm{D}}_{h}:h\in[m]\}
in (
2.8
)
[
6
, Theorem 2.8]
, and
the same asymptotic analysis goes through with minimal modification if we replace the matrices
\{\bm{D}_{h}:h\in[m]\}
with the estimated signal strength matrices
\{\widehat{\bm{D}}_{h}:h\in[m]\}
.
.
The last two parts of Assumption
5.1
are satisfied by many commonly considered classes of priors such as priors supported on compact subsets of
\mathbb{R}^{r+\widetilde{r}}
and
\mathbb{R}^{r_{h}}
, respectively, as well as Gaussian mixture models with bounded component means and component covariances bounded away from zero.

5.2
Asymptotic properties of OrchAMP estimators

5.2.1
Estimation of nuisance parameters and priors

A function
\psi:\mathbb{R}^{p}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
is called
pseudo-Lipschitz
if there exists a constant
C>0
such that for any
\bm{x},\bm{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
we have

\displaystyle|\psi(\bm{x})-\psi(\bm{y})|\leq C\left(1+\|\bm{x}\|+\|\bm{y}\|\right)\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|.

(5.6)

In the super-critical regime, the matrices
\{{\bm{U}}^{\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h},{\bm{V}}^{\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}:h\in[m]\}
, satisfy the following properties.

Proposition 5.1
.

For all
h\in[m]
, consider the matrices
\widebar{\bm{X}}_{h}
and their best rank
r_{h}
approximations given by
\frac{1}{N}{\bm{U}}^{\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}\bm{D}_{0,h}({\bm{V}}^{\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h})^{\top}
.
Let the matrices
\{\bm{S}^{L,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h},\bm{\Sigma}^{L,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h},\bm{S}^{R,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bm{\Sigma}^{R,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}}:h\in[m]\}
be defined by (
2.6
) and (
2.7
).
Then, for any sequence of deterministic subsets
\mathcal{F}_{L}\subset[N]
satisfying
\frac{|\mathcal{F}_{L}|}{N}\rightarrow\lambda_{L}\in(0,1]
, as
N\rightarrow\infty
, and any pseudo-Lipschitz function
\psi:\mathbb{R}^{2(r+\widetilde{r})}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}

as defined in (
5.6
)
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{|\mathcal{F}_{L}|}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{F}_{L}}\psi\big(({\bm{U}}^{\mathrm{pc}}_{0,1})_{i*},\dots,({\bm{U}}^{\mathrm{pc}}_{0,m})_{i*},(\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{1})_{i*},\dots,(\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{\widetilde{m}})_{i*},

(5.7)

\displaystyle(\bm{U}_{1})_{i*},\ldots,(\bm{U}_{m})_{i*},(\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{1})_{i*},\ldots,(\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{\widetilde{m}})_{i*}\big)

\displaystyle\overset{a.s.}{=}\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\left[\psi(Y_{0,1},\ldots,Y_{0,m},\widetilde{Y}_{0,1},\ldots,\widetilde{Y}_{0,\widetilde{m}},U_{1},\ldots,U_{m},\widetilde{U}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{U}_{\widetilde{m}})\right].

Here,

\{Y_{0,h}:h\in[m]\}
and
\{\widetilde{Y}_{0,\ell}:\ell\in[\widetilde{m}]\}
are defined by (
2.11
) and the collection

(U_{1},\ldots,U_{m},\widetilde{U}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{U}_{\widetilde{m}})\sim\mu
.
Furthermore, conditional on
(U_{1},\ldots,U_{m},\widetilde{U}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{U}_{\widetilde{m}})
, the collection of random vectors
\{Y_{0,1},\ldots,Y_{0,m},\widetilde{Y}_{0,1},\ldots,\widetilde{Y}_{0,\widetilde{m}}\}
are mutually independent.
Similarly, for all
h\in[m]
, if we consider any sequence of deterministic subsets
\mathcal{F}_{R,h}\subset[p_{h}]
that satisfy
\frac{|\mathcal{F}_{R,h}|}{p_{h}}\rightarrow\lambda_{R,h}\in(0,1]
, as
p_{h}\rightarrow\infty
and any pseudo-Lipschitz function
\varphi_{h}:\mathbb{R}^{2r_{h}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{p_{h}\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{|\mathcal{F}_{R,h}|}\sum_{j\in\mathcal{F}_{R,h}}\varphi_{h}(({\bm{V}}^{\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h})_{j*},(\bm{V}_{h})_{j*})\overset{a.s.}{=}\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{h}}\left[\varphi_{h}(Y^{R}_{0,h},V_{h})\right],

(5.8)

where
\{Y^{R}_{0,h}:h\in[m]\}
is defined by (
2.14
) and
V_{h}\sim\nu_{h}
.

This proposition substantiates the approximation in (
2.5
) and lays the theoretical foundation for the empirical Bayes estimation of priors
described in (
2.13
) and (
2.15
).

Remark 5.1
.

The expectations on the right hand sides of (
5.7
) and (
5.8
) are with respect to the priors
\mu
and
\{\nu_{h}:h\in[m]\}
and the Gaussian measures used in defining
\{Y_{0,h},Y^{R}_{0,h}:h\in[m]\}
and
\{\widetilde{Y}_{0,\ell}:\ell\in[\widetilde{m}]\}
.
Thus, the quantities on the right sides are deterministic.
We have deliberately suppressed the Gaussian measures in the subscript of the expectation for conciseness.
A similar convention is followed throughout the manuscript.

Next, let us consider the estimates of the nuisance parameters used to initialize Algorithm
1
, defined in (
2.8
), (
2.9
) and (
2.10
). The following lemma shows that these estimates are consistent estimators of the population quantities
\{\bm{D}_{h}:h\in[m]\}
,
\{\bm{L}_{\ell}:\ell\in[\widetilde{m}]\}
and
\{\bm{S}^{\star,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h},\bm{\Sigma}^{\star,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}:\star\in\{L,R\},\,h\in[m]\}
defined in (
1.1
), (
1.2
) and (
2.7
).

Lemma 5.1
.

Under Assumptions
5.1
, the following properties hold as
N\rightarrow\infty
:

1.

For all
h\in[m]
,

\widehat{\bm{D}}_{h}\xrightarrow{a.s}\bm{D}_{h},

where
\widehat{\bm{D}}_{h}\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{h}\times r_{h}}
is defined in (
2.8
).

2.

For all
\ell\in[\widetilde{m}]
, the estimator of
\bm{L}_{\ell}
defined in (
2.10
) satisfies

\widehat{\bm{L}}_{\ell}\xrightarrow{a.s.}\bm{L}_{\ell}.

3.

For all
h\in[m]
and
\star\in\{L,R\}
,
\widehat{\bm{S}}^{\star,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}\xrightarrow{a.s}{\bm{S}}^{\star,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}
and
\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\star,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}\xrightarrow{a.s}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\star,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}
, where the estimators
\widehat{\bm{S}}^{\star,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h},\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\star,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}
are defined in (
2.9
).

Finally, consider the estimated priors
\widehat{\mu}
and
\{\widehat{\nu}_{h}:h\in[m]\}
defined in (
2.13
) and (
2.15
), respectively. The following lemma shows that they converge to the true data generating priors
\mu
and
\{\nu_{h}:h\in[m]\}
as
N\rightarrow\infty
if the nonparametric classes are properly chosen.

Lemma 5.2
.

If
\mu\in\mathcal{P}
and
\nu_{h}\in\mathcal{P}_{\nu_{h}}
for all
h\in[m]
; then as
N\rightarrow\infty
,
\widehat{\mu}\overset{w}{\rightarrow}\mu
and
\widehat{\nu}_{h}\overset{w}{\rightarrow}\nu_{h}
, almost surely.

5.2.2
Asymptotics of OrchAMP iterates

To analyze the asymptotic behavior of OrchAMP iterates (
2.16
) and (
2.22
),
we begin with their oracle counterparts
constructed using the true priors
\mu
and
\{\nu_{h}:h\in[m]\}
.
Define

\bm{S}^{L,\circ}_{t,h}
,
\bm{\Sigma}^{L,\circ}_{t,h}
,
\bm{S}^{R,\circ}_{t,h}
and
\bm{\Sigma}^{R,\circ}_{t,h}\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{h}\times r_{h}}
, for all integers
t\geq 0
, recursively as follows:

\displaystyle\bm{\Sigma}^{L,\circ}_{t,h}

\displaystyle=\gamma_{h}\;\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{h}}[v^{\circ}_{t,h}(Y^{R,\circ}_{t,h};\nu_{h})^{\otimes 2}],

(5.9)

\displaystyle\bm{S}^{L,\circ}_{t,h}

\displaystyle=\gamma_{h}\;\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{h}}[v^{\circ}_{t,h}(Y^{R,\circ}_{t,h};\nu_{h})V^{\top}_{h}]\bm{D}_{h},

\displaystyle\bm{\Sigma}^{R,\circ}_{t+1,h}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[u^{\circ}_{t,h}(Y^{\circ}_{t,1},\ldots,Y^{\circ}_{t,m},\widetilde{Y}_{0,1},\ldots,\widetilde{Y}_{0,\widetilde{m}};\mu)^{\otimes 2}],

\displaystyle\bm{S}^{R,\circ}_{t+1,h}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[u^{\circ}_{t,h}(Y^{\circ}_{t,1},\ldots,Y^{\circ}_{t,m},\widetilde{Y}_{0,1},\ldots,\widetilde{Y}_{0,\widetilde{m}};\mu)U^{\top}_{h}]\bm{D}_{h},

with
\bm{\Sigma}^{R,\circ}_{0,h}=\bm{\Sigma}^{R,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}
and
\bm{S}^{R,\circ}_{0,h}=\bm{S}^{R,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}
.
In the above equations, for all
h\in[m]
and
\ell\in[\widetilde{m}]
,

\displaystyle Y^{\circ}_{t,h}\sim N_{r_{h}}(\bm{S}^{L,\circ}_{t,h}U_{h},\bm{\Sigma}^{L,\circ}_{t,h})\quad\mbox{and}\quad\widetilde{Y}_{0,\ell}\sim N_{{\widetilde{r}}_{h}}(\bm{L}_{\ell}\widetilde{U}_{\ell},\bm{I}_{\widetilde{r}_{\ell}}),

(5.10)

with
(U_{1},\ldots,U_{m},\widetilde{U}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{U}_{\widetilde{m}})\sim\mu
, and conditional on
(U_{1},\ldots,U_{m},\widetilde{U}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{U}_{\widetilde{m}})
, the elements in the collection
\{Y^{\circ}_{t,1},\ldots,Y^{\circ}_{t,m},\widetilde{Y}_{0,1},\ldots,\widetilde{Y}_{0,\widetilde{m}}\}
are mutually independent.
Similarly, for all
h\in[m]
,

Y^{R,\circ}_{t,h}\sim N_{r_{h}}(\bm{S}^{R,\circ}_{t,h}V_{h},\bm{\Sigma}^{R,\circ}_{t,h}),\;\mbox{where}\;V_{h}\sim\nu_{h}.

(5.11)

In addition, for
h\in[m]
and
\ell\in[\widetilde{m}]
, the
oracle denoisers
,
\{v^{\circ}_{t,h}\}:\mathbb{R}^{r_{h}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}r_{h}}}
,
\{u^{\circ}_{t,h}\}:\mathbb{R}^{r+{\widetilde{r}}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{r_{h}}
and
\{\widetilde{u}^{\circ}_{t,\ell}\}:\mathbb{R}^{r+{\widetilde{r}}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{\widetilde{r}_{\ell}}
, are defined as follows:

\displaystyle v^{\circ}_{t,h}(x;\nu_{h})=\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{h}}[V_{h}|Y^{R,\circ}_{t,h}=x]

(5.12)

\displaystyle u^{\circ}_{t,h}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m},\widetilde{x}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{x}_{\widetilde{m}};\mu)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[U_{h}|Y^{\circ}_{t,1}=x_{1},\ldots,Y^{\circ}_{t,m}=x_{m},\widetilde{Y}_{0,1}=\widetilde{x}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{Y}_{0,\widetilde{m}}=\widetilde{x}_{\widetilde{m}}],

\displaystyle\widetilde{u}^{\circ}_{t,\ell}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m},\widetilde{x}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{x}_{\widetilde{m}};\mu)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[\widetilde{U}_{\ell}|Y^{\circ}_{t,1}=x_{1},\ldots,Y^{\circ}_{t,m}=x_{m},\widetilde{Y}_{0,1}=\widetilde{x}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{Y}_{0,\widetilde{m}}=\widetilde{x}_{\widetilde{m}}].

Now, for
h\in[m]
consider the set of iterates,
\{\widebar{\bm{U}}^{\circ}_{t,h},\widebar{\bm{V}}^{\circ}_{t,h}\}_{t\geq 0}
given by:

\displaystyle\widebar{\bm{V}}^{\circ}_{t,h}=v^{\circ}_{t,h}({\bm{V}}^{\circ}_{t,h};\nu_{h}),

(5.13)

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\bm{U}}^{\circ}_{t,h}=\widebar{\bm{X}}_{h}\widebar{\bm{V}}^{\circ}_{t,h}-\gamma_{h}\widebar{\bm{U}}^{\circ}_{t-1,h}(\bm{J}^{R,\circ}_{t,h}(\bm{V}^{\circ}_{t,h};\nu_{h}))^{\top}

\displaystyle\widebar{\bm{U}}^{\circ}_{t,h}=u^{\circ}_{t,h}({\bm{U}}^{\circ}_{t,1},\ldots,{\bm{U}}^{\circ}_{t,m},\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{\widetilde{m}};\mu),

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\bm{V}}^{\circ}_{t+1,h}=\widebar{\bm{X}}^{\top}_{h}\widebar{\bm{U}}^{\circ}_{t,h}-\widebar{\bm{V}}^{\circ}_{t,h}(\bm{J}^{L,\circ}_{t,h}(\bm{U}^{\circ}_{t,1},\ldots,\bm{U}^{\circ}_{t,m},\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{\widetilde{m}};\mu))^{\top},

where
\widebar{\bm{U}}^{\circ}_{-1,h}=\widebar{\bm{U}}_{-1,h}
and
\bm{V}^{\circ}_{0,h}=\bm{V}_{0,h}
.
Here, the matrices

u^{\circ}_{t,h}(\bm{U}^{\circ}_{t,1},\ldots,\bm{U}^{\circ}_{t,m},\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{\widetilde{m}};\mu)\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times r_{h}}\quad\mbox{and}\quad v^{\circ}_{t,h}(\bm{V}^{\circ}_{t,h};\nu_{h})\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{h}\times r_{h}}

are constructed by applying
u^{\circ}_{t,h}
to the collection of rows of the embeddings
(\bm{U}^{\circ}_{t,1},\ldots,\bm{U}^{\circ}_{t,m},\newline
\widetilde{\bm{X}}^{\circ}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{\bm{X}}^{\circ}_{\widetilde{m}})
and
v^{\circ}_{t,h}
to the rows of
\bm{V}^{\circ}_{t,h}
, respectively, as in (
2.19
).
Further, the matrix
\bm{J}^{R,\circ}_{t,h}(\bm{V}^{\circ}_{t,h};\nu_{h})\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{h}\times r_{h}}
is defined entrywise as follows: For
(i,j)\in[r_{h}]\times[r_{h}]
,

\displaystyle\left[\bm{J}^{R,\circ}_{t,h}(\bm{V}^{\circ}_{t,h};\nu_{h})\right]_{ij}=\frac{1}{p_{h}}\sum_{k=1}^{p_{h}}\frac{\partial v^{\circ}_{t,h,i}}{\partial x_{j}}\left((\bm{V}^{\circ}_{t,h})_{k*};\nu_{h}\right),

(5.14)

where
v^{\circ}_{t,h}(\cdot)=(v^{\circ}_{t,h,1}(\cdot),\ldots,v^{\circ}_{t,h,r_{h}}(\cdot))^{\top}
and
\partial v^{\circ}_{t,h,i}/\partial x_{j}
is the partial derivative of
v^{\circ}_{t,h,i}
with respect to its
j
-th argument. Similarly, for
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m},\widetilde{x}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{x}_{\widetilde{m}})\in\mathbb{R}^{r+\widetilde{r}}
, let
\bm{J}^{\circ}_{t,h}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m},\widetilde{x}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{x}_{\widetilde{m}};\mu)
be the Jacobian of the function
u^{\circ}_{t,h}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m},\widetilde{x}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{x}_{\widetilde{m}};\mu)
and
[\bm{J}^{\circ}_{t,h}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m},\widetilde{x}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{x}_{\widetilde{m}};\mu)]_{*\mathcal{I}_{h}}
be the submatrix of
\bm{J}^{\circ}_{t,h}
formed by selecting the columns in
\mathcal{I}_{h}
, where
\mathcal{I}_{h}
is defined in (
2.20
).
Then, the matrix
\bm{J}^{L,\circ}_{t,h}

in the last line of (
5.13
) is defined as

\displaystyle\bm{J}^{L,\circ}_{t,h}(\bm{U}^{\circ}_{t,1},\ldots,\bm{U}^{\circ}_{t,m},\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{\widetilde{m}};\mu)

(5.15)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}[\bm{J}^{\circ}_{t,h}((\bm{U}^{\circ}_{t,1})_{k*},\ldots,(\bm{U}^{\circ}_{t,m})_{k*},(\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{1})_{k*},\ldots,(\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{\widetilde{m}})_{k*};\mu)]_{*\mathcal{I}_{h}},

Finally,
for
\ell\in[\widetilde{m}]
and all
t\geq 0
, define

\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{\circ}_{t,\ell}=\widetilde{u}^{\circ}_{t,\ell}({\bm{U}}^{\circ}_{t,1},\ldots,{\bm{U}}^{\circ}_{t,m},\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{\widetilde{m}};\mu),

where the matrices
\widetilde{u}^{\circ}_{t,\ell}({\bm{U}}^{\circ}_{t,1},\ldots,{\bm{U}}^{\circ}_{t,m},\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{\widetilde{m}};\mu)\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times\widetilde{r}_{\ell}}
are constructed by applying the function
\widetilde{u}^{\circ}_{t,\ell}
rowwise to the collection of matrices
{\bm{U}}^{\circ}_{t,1},\ldots,{\bm{U}}^{\circ}_{t,m}
and
\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{\widetilde{m}}
as in (
2.23
).

With the aid of the foregoing definitions of oracle quantities, the following theorem characterizes the large sample asymptotics of the (empirical Bayes) OrchAMP iterates.

Theorem 5.1
.

Under Assumption
5.1
, we have the following for all
t\geq 0
:

1.

As
N\rightarrow\infty
, for all
h\in[m]
,

\frac{1}{N}\|\bm{U}_{t,h}-{\bm{U}}^{\circ}_{t,h}\|^{2}_{F}\xrightarrow{a.s.}0
,

\frac{1}{p_{h}}\|\bm{V}_{t,h}-{\bm{V}}^{\circ}_{t,h}\|^{2}_{F}\xrightarrow{a.s.}0
,
and for all
\ell\in[\widetilde{m}]
,

\frac{1}{N}\|\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{t,\ell}-\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{\circ}_{t,\ell}\|^{2}_{F}\xrightarrow{a.s.}0
.

2.

As
N\rightarrow\infty
, for all
h\in[m]
,

\widehat{\bm{S}}^{\star}_{t,h}\xrightarrow{a.s.}{\bm{S}}^{\star,\circ}_{t,h}
,
and

\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\star}_{t,h}\xrightarrow{a.s.}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\star,\circ}_{t,h}
,

for
\star\in\{L,R\}
.

3.

For any sequence of deterministic subsets
\mathcal{F}_{L}\subset[N]
satisfying
\frac{|\mathcal{F}_{L}|}{N}\rightarrow\lambda_{L}\in(0,1]
as
N\rightarrow\infty
, and any pseudo-Lipschitz function
\psi:\mathbb{R}^{2(r+\widetilde{r})}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{|\mathcal{F}_{L}|}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{F}_{L}}\psi\Big((\bm{U}_{t,1})_{i*},\ldots,(\bm{U}_{t,m})_{i*},(\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{1})_{i*},\dots,(\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{\widetilde{m}})_{i*},

(5.16)

\displaystyle(\bm{U}_{1})_{i*},\ldots,(\bm{U}_{m})_{i*},(\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{1})_{i*},\ldots,(\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{\widetilde{m}})_{i*}\Big)

\displaystyle\overset{a.s.}{=}\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\left[\psi(Y^{\circ}_{t,1},\ldots,Y^{\circ}_{t,m},\widetilde{Y}_{0,1},\ldots,\widetilde{Y}_{0,\widetilde{m}},U_{1},\ldots,U_{m},\tilde{U}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{U}_{\widetilde{m}})\right],

where
(U_{1},\ldots,U_{m},\widetilde{U}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{U}_{\widetilde{m}})\sim\mu
. Here,
\{Y^{\circ}_{t,h}:h\in[m]\}
and
\{\widetilde{Y}_{0,\ell}:\ell\in[\widetilde{m}]\}
are defined in (
5.10
).
Furthermore, for all
h\in[m]
, if we consider any sequence of deterministic subsets
\mathcal{F}_{R,h}\subset[p_{h}]
that satisfy
\frac{|\mathcal{F}_{R,h}|}{p_{h}}\rightarrow\lambda_{R,h}\in(0,1]
as
p_{h}\rightarrow\infty
, and any pseudo-Lipschitz function
\varphi_{h}:\mathbb{R}^{2r_{h}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
, we have,

\displaystyle\lim_{p_{h}\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{|\mathcal{F}_{R,h}|}\sum_{j\in\mathcal{F}_{R,h}}\varphi_{h}((\bm{V}_{t,h})_{j*},(\bm{V}_{h})_{j*})\overset{a.s.}{=}\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{h}}\left[\varphi_{h}(Y^{R,\circ}_{t,h},V_{h})\right],

(5.17)

where
V_{h}\sim\nu_{h}
and
Y^{R,\circ}_{t,h}
is defined in (
5.11
).

Considering
\mathcal{F}_{L}=[N]
and
\mathcal{F}_{R,h}=[p_{h}]
for all
h\in[m]
, we can synopsize the last claim of the above theorem as follows:

\displaystyle\bm{U}_{t,h}\approx\bm{U}_{h}(\widehat{\bm{S}}^{L}_{t,h})^{\top}+\bm{Z}^{L}_{h}(\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}^{L}_{t,h})^{1/2}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\bm{V}_{t,h}\approx\bm{V}_{h}(\widehat{\bm{S}}^{R}_{t,h})^{\top}+{\bm{Z}^{R}_{h}}(\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}^{R}_{t,h})^{1/2},

(5.18)

where
\bm{Z}^{L}_{h}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times r_{h}}
and
\bm{Z}^{R}_{h}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{h}\times r_{h}}
are matrices with iid
N(0,1)

entries
for all
h\in[m]
.

Theorem
5.1
implies the following corollary characterizing the mean-squared-error (MSE)
risk of estimating
\{\bm{U}_{h},\bm{V}_{h}:h\in[m]\}
with
\{\widebar{\bm{U}}_{t,h},\widebar{\bm{V}}_{t,h}:h\in[m]\}
.

Corollary 5.1
.

Under Assumption
5.1
,
for all
t\geq 0
, the iterates
\{\widebar{\bm{U}}_{t,h},\widebar{\bm{V}}_{t,h}:h\in[m]\}
satisfy:

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{N^{2}}\|\widebar{\bm{U}}_{t,h}\widebar{\bm{U}}^{\top}_{t,h}-\bm{U}_{h}\bm{U}^{\top}_{h}\|^{2}_{F}

\displaystyle\,\overset{a.s}{=}\,\mathrm{Tr}\left(\bm{I}_{r_{h}}-(\bm{\Sigma}^{R,\circ}_{t+1,h})^{2}\right),

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{p_{h}\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{p^{2}_{h}}\|\widebar{\bm{V}}_{t,h}\widebar{\bm{V}}^{\top}_{t,h}-\bm{V}_{h}\bm{V}^{\top}_{h}\|^{2}_{F}

\displaystyle\,\overset{a.s}{=}\,\mathrm{Tr}\left(\bm{I}_{r_{h}}-\frac{1}{\gamma^{2}_{h}}{(\bm{\Sigma}^{L,\circ}_{t,h})^{2}}\right).

(5.19)

The counterpart for estimating
\{\bm{\widetilde{U}}_{\ell}:\ell\in[\widetilde{m}]\}
with
\{\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{t,\ell}:\ell\in[\widetilde{m}]\}
are as follows.

Corollary 5.2
.

Under Assumption
5.1
, for all
t\geq 0
,
the iterates
\{\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{t,\ell}:\ell\in[\widetilde{m}]\}
satisfy:

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{N^{2}}\|\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{t,\ell}\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{\top}_{t,\ell}-\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{\ell}\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{\top}_{\ell}\|^{2}_{F}\,\overset{a.s.}{=}\,\mathrm{Tr}\left(\bm{I}_{\widetilde{r}_{\ell}}-(\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\circ}_{t,\ell})^{2}\right),

(5.20)

where

\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\circ}_{t,\ell}=\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\left[\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[\widetilde{U}_{\ell}\,\Big|\,Y^{\circ}_{t,1},\ldots,Y^{\circ}_{t,m},\widetilde{Y}_{0,1},\ldots,\widetilde{Y}_{0,m}]^{\otimes 2}\right]
.

5.2.3
Bayes optimality of OrchAMP estimators

Fix any set of positive definite matrices
\{\bm{B}^{L}_{h},\bm{B}^{R}_{h}\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{h}\times r_{h}}:h\in[m]\}
.
Conditional on the latent factors
({U}_{1},\ldots,{U}_{m},\widetilde{U}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{U}_{\widetilde{m}})\sim\mu
and
V_{h}\sim\nu_{h}
for
h\in[m]
, define random vectors
\mathrm{X}^{L}_{h}=\mathrm{X}^{L}_{h}(\bm{B}^{L}_{h})
,
\mathrm{X}^{R}_{h}=\mathrm{X}^{R}_{h}(\bm{B}^{R}_{h})
,
h\in[m]
and
\widetilde{\mathrm{X}}^{L}_{\ell}
,
\ell\in[{\widetilde{m}}]
as

\mathrm{X}^{L}_{h}(\bm{B}^{L}_{h})\sim N_{r_{h}}(U_{h},(\bm{B}^{L}_{h})^{-1}),\,\,\mathrm{X}^{R}_{h}(\bm{B}^{R}_{h})\sim N_{r_{h}}(V_{h},(\bm{B}^{R}_{h})^{-1}),\,\,\widetilde{\mathrm{X}}^{L}_{\ell}\sim N_{{\widetilde{r}}_{\ell}}(\bm{L}_{\ell}\widetilde{U}_{\ell},\bm{I}_{\widetilde{r}_{\ell}}).

(5.21)

Next, for all
h\in[m]
, define the matrices
\mathsf{u}_{h}(\bm{B}^{L}_{1},\ldots,\bm{B}^{L}_{m};\mu)\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{h}\times r_{h}}
and
\mathsf{v}_{h}(\bm{B}^{R}_{h};\nu_{h})\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{h}\times r_{h}}
as follows:

\displaystyle\mathsf{u}_{h}(\bm{B}^{L}_{1},\ldots,\bm{B}^{L}_{m};\mu)

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[(U_{h}-\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[U_{h}|\mathrm{X}^{L}_{1}(\bm{B}^{L}_{1}),\ldots,\mathrm{X}^{L}_{m}(\bm{B}^{L}_{m}),\widetilde{\mathrm{X}}^{L}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{\mathrm{X}}^{L}_{\widetilde{m}}])^{\otimes 2}],

(5.22)

\displaystyle\mathsf{v}_{h}(\bm{B}^{R}_{h};\nu_{h})

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{h}}[(V_{h}-\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{h}}[V_{h}|\mathrm{X}^{R}_{h}(\bm{B}^{R}_{h})])^{\otimes 2}],

(5.23)

and
matrices
\bm{\Gamma}^{R}_{t,h},\bm{\Gamma}^{L}_{t,h}\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{h}\times r_{h}}
as follows:

\displaystyle\bm{\Gamma}^{L}_{t,h}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{\gamma_{h}}\bm{D}^{-1/2}_{h}(\bm{S}^{L,\circ}_{t,h})^{\top}(\bm{\Sigma}^{L,\circ}_{t,h})^{-1}\bm{S}^{L,\circ}_{t,h}\bm{D}^{-1/2}_{h},

(5.24)

\displaystyle\bm{\Gamma}^{R}_{t,h}

\displaystyle:=\bm{D}^{-1/2}_{h}(\bm{S}^{R,\circ}_{t,h})^{\top}(\bm{\Sigma}^{R,\circ}_{t,h})^{-1}\bm{S}^{R,\circ}_{t,h}\bm{D}^{-1/2}_{h}.

Using the foregoing definitions, we
can reformulate

the
state evolution
recursions
as follows
.

Lemma 5.3
.

The state evolution recursions (
5.9
) can be equivalently characterized
as

\displaystyle\bm{\Gamma}^{L}_{t,h}=\bm{D}_{h}-\mathsf{v}_{h}(\bm{\Gamma}^{R}_{t,h};\bm{D}^{1/2}_{h}\nu_{h}),\quad\bm{\Gamma}^{R}_{t+1,h}=\bm{D}_{h}-\mathsf{u}_{h}(\gamma_{1}\bm{\Gamma}^{L}_{t,1},\ldots,\gamma_{m}\bm{\Gamma}^{L}_{t,m};\bm{D}^{1/2}\mu),

(5.25)

where
\bm{D}=\mathsf{diag}(\bm{D}_{1},\ldots,\bm{D}_{m},\bm{I}_{\widetilde{r}_{1}},\ldots,\bm{I}_{\widetilde{r}_{\widetilde{m}}})
.

The following theorem characterizes the large-
t
asymptotics of the state evolutions.

Theorem 5.2
.

There exists matrices
\bm{\Gamma}^{R}_{\infty,h},\bm{\Gamma}^{L}_{\infty,h}\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{h}\times r_{h}}
for
h\in[m]
, satisfying

\displaystyle\bm{\Gamma}^{L}_{\infty,h}=\bm{D}_{h}-\mathsf{v}_{h}(\bm{\Gamma}^{R}_{\infty,h};\bm{D}^{1/2}_{h}\nu_{h}),\,\bm{\Gamma}^{R}_{\infty,h}=\bm{D}_{h}-\mathsf{u}_{h}(\gamma_{1}\bm{\Gamma}^{L}_{\infty,1},\ldots,\gamma_{m}\bm{\Gamma}^{L}_{\infty,m};\bm{D}^{1/2}\mu),

(5.26)

such that
\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\bm{\Gamma}^{L}_{t,h}=\bm{\Gamma}^{L}_{\infty,h}
and
\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\bm{\Gamma}^{R}_{t,h}=\bm{\Gamma}^{R}_{\infty,h}
, for all
h\in[m]
.

Remark 5.2
.

The system of equations (
5.26
) is guaranteed to have at least one set of fixed points
\bm{\Gamma}^{R}_{\infty,h}
and
\bm{\Gamma}^{L}_{\infty,h}
, while the set of fixed points is not necessarily unique.
Whether the set is
unique is determined by the specific choice of the priors
\mu
and
\{\nu_{h}:h\in[m]\}
.

Next, consider the Bayes estimators of the signals
\bm{U}_{h}\bm{D}_{h}\bm{V}^{\top}_{h}
for
h\in[m]
. The I-MMSE identity as in Proposition 3 of
[
38
]

implies that

\displaystyle\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{Np_{h}}\mathbb{E}[\|\bm{U}_{h}\bm{D}_{h}\bm{V}^{\top}_{h}-\mathbb{E}[\bm{U}_{h}\bm{D}_{h}\bm{V}^{\top}_{h}|\bm{X}_{1},\ldots,\bm{X}_{m},\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{\widetilde{m}}]\|^{2}_{F}]

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Tr}(\bm{D}^{2}_{h})-\mathrm{Tr}\,(\bm{\Gamma}^{L}_{\star,h}\bm{\Gamma}^{R}_{\star,h}),

for some fixed point
\{(\bm{\Gamma}^{L}_{\star,h}\bm{\Gamma}^{R}_{\star,h}):h\in[m]\}
of the recursion (
5.25
).

Though the estimators
\mathbb{E}[\bm{U}_{h}\bm{D}_{h}\bm{V}^{\top}_{h}|\bm{X}_{1},\ldots,\bm{X}_{m},\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{\widetilde{m}}]
give the optimal
MSE
risk for estimating
\bm{U}_{h}\bm{D}_{h}\bm{V}^{\top}_{h}
,
they may not be computationally tractable (in the sense of polynomial time complexity) for
general
priors
\mu
and
\{\nu_{h}:h\in[m]\}
.
In contrast, the following theorem shows that after sufficiently many iterative refinements of the empirical singular vectors using
(
2.16
), the reconstruction error of the OrchAMP estimators
\widebar{\bm{U}}_{t,h}\bm{D}_{h}\widebar{\bm{V}}^{\top}_{t,h}
for any
h\in[m]
is
asymptotically
equal to the Bayes optimal risk as long as
\bm{\Gamma}^{L}_{\infty,h}
and
\bm{\Gamma}^{R}_{\infty,h}
are unique.

Theorem 5.3
.

If there exists unique fixed points of the recursive equations (
5.26
) for the priors
\mu
and
\{\nu_{h}:h\in[m]\}
given by
\{\bm{\Gamma}^{L}_{\infty,h},\bm{\Gamma}^{R}_{\infty,h}:h\in[m]\}
, then
the OrchAMP estimators
\{\widebar{\bm{U}}_{t,h}\bm{D}_{h}\widebar{\bm{V}}^{\top}_{t,h}:h\in[m]\}
satisfy
that for all
h\in[m]
,

\displaystyle\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{Np_{h}}\|\bm{U}_{h}\bm{D}_{h}\bm{V}^{\top}_{h}-\widebar{\bm{U}}_{t,h}\bm{D}_{h}\widebar{\bm{V}}^{\top}_{t,h}\|^{2}_{F}\overset{a.s.}{=}\mathrm{Tr}(\bm{D}^{2}_{h})-\mathrm{Tr}\,(\bm{\Gamma}^{L}_{\infty,h}\bm{\Gamma}^{R}_{\infty,h}).

(5.27)

We refer interested readers to
[
3
,
39
,
14
]
for examples of priors with unique fixed points when
h=1,\ell=0
and
r_{h}=1
. Further, one can find an example of a set of priors with unique fixed points when
h=2,\ell=0
and
r_{1}=r_{2}=1
in
[
34
]
.

5.3
Asymptotic coverage of prediction sets

To begin with, let us characterize the asymptotic distributions of
\{\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{Q},0}_{h_{k}}:k\in[d]\}
defined in (
2.26
).

Theorem 5.4
.

Consider the predictors
\{\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{Q},0}_{h_{k}}:k\in[d]\}
defined in (
2.27
).
Suppose as
N\rightarrow\infty
,
|\mathcal{F}_{h_{k}}|/p_{h_{k}}\rightarrow\lambda_{h_{k}}\in(0,1]
for
k\in[d]
.
Then there exist
Z_{h_{k}}\sim N_{r_{h_{k}}}(0,{\bm{I}}_{r_{h_{k}}})
,
k\in[d]
, which are mutually independent and independent of
({U}_{1}^{\mathrm{Q}},\dots,{U}_{m}^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{U}_{1}^{\mathrm{Q}},\dots,\widetilde{U}_{\widetilde{m}}^{\mathrm{Q}})
, such that conditional on
({U}_{1}^{\mathrm{Q}},\dots,{U}_{m}^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{U}_{1}^{\mathrm{Q}},\dots,\widetilde{U}_{\widetilde{m}}^{\mathrm{Q}})
,
we have jointly over
k\in[d]
,

\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{Q},0}_{h_{k}}\,\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{\to}}\,U^{\mathrm{Q}}_{h_{k}}+\lambda^{-1/2}_{h_{k}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left(\bm{D}^{-1}_{h_{k}}(\bm{\Sigma}^{L,\circ}_{T,h_{k}})^{-1}\bm{D}^{-1}_{h_{k}}\right)^{1/2}}Z_{h_{k}}.

To obtain the asymptotic coverage probability for the prediction set of the query’s full latent representation

(U^{\mathrm{Q}}_{1},\ldots,U^{\mathrm{Q}}_{m},\widetilde{U}^{\mathrm{Q}}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{U}^{\mathrm{Q}}_{\widetilde{m}})
,
we establish the following lemma proving that the class of closed convex subsets of
\mathbb{R}^{r+\widetilde{r}}
, denoted by
\mathscr{C}_{r+\widetilde{r}}
,
is Glivenko–Cantelli
with respect to the sequence of measures
\widehat{\mu}
that converges weakly to the true underlying prior
\mu
.

Lemma 5.4
.

If
\mu\in\mathcal{P}
, then as
N\rightarrow\infty
,

\sup_{A\in\mathscr{C}_{r+\widetilde{r}}}|\mu(A)-{\widehat{\mu}}(A)|\xrightarrow{a.s.}0
.

Since for any
\alpha\in(0,1)
, our prediction set
\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}
, by definition, is a closed convex subset of
\mathbb{R}^{r+\widetilde{r}}
, Lemma
5.4
implies the following theorem.

Theorem 5.5
.

Suppose that
\mu\in\mathcal{P}
and that
\widehat{\mu}
is any sequence of priors converging to
\mu
weakly as
N\rightarrow\infty

For any
\alpha\in(0,1)
, the nominal
100\times(1-\alpha)\%
prediction set
\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}
defined in (
2.34
) satisfies

\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{P}_{\mu}\left[(U^{\mathrm{Q}}_{1},\ldots,U^{\mathrm{Q}}_{m},\widetilde{U}^{\mathrm{Q}}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{U}^{\mathrm{Q}}_{\widetilde{m}})\in\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}\right]\geq 1-\alpha.

Appendix B
Benchmarking atlas construction methods on a CITE-seq data example

To further benchmark OrchAMP against state-of-the-art atlas construction methods for multi-omics data, we present an additional CITE-seq data example which has two modalities (RNA, with roughly
20000
transcripts, and Protein, with
224
surface proteins), which consists of
10000
peripheral blood mononuclear cells (PBMCs) randomly sampled from the large-scale CITE-seq dataset in
Hao et al., [23]
after
standard pre-processing
recommended in the source study.

We retained
5000
highly variable genes and
30
highly variable protein markers for our analysis.

Naturally, RNA is the high-dimensional modality and Protein low-dimensional.

When the data was initially collected, cells were processed across eight lanes of a 10x Genomics Chip B, which introduced noticeable batch effects.
We employed Harmony
[
29
]

to remove lane-specific artifacts in both modalities.

In addition to WNN and MOFA+, we also included in our comparison below totalVI
[
19
]
, which is an atlas construction method initially designed specifically for CITE-seq data.
Harmony-corrected values were supplied to all atlas construction methods except for totalVI, which has a built-in batch correction capacity and only requires batch indices as part of its input.

B.1
Cell atlas construction

Based on a comparison between the empirical singular value distribution and the appropriately rescaled Marchenko–Pastur law, we had set the latent dimension of the high-dimensional modality to
r_{1}=50
. We then adopted an Empirical Bayes approach to estimate the priors:
\mu
for the concatenated rows of
\bm{U}_{1}
and
\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{1}
, and the rows of
\bm{V}
. Both priors were modeled using Gaussian mixtures, with
8
mixture components for
\mu
and
4
for
\nu
. These choices were determined by the same procedure as described in Section 4, and were used in the OrchAMP atlas construction reported below.

Figure
6
plots the UMAP representations
[
35
]
of the OrchAMP atlas (top right), alongside the UMAP representations obtained from three other methods: the WNN-based integration of
Hao et al., [23]
(top left), using 50 principal components for the RNA modality and 30 for the protein modality, MOFA+
[
1
]
(bottom right) with 20 latent factors, and totalVI
[
19
]
(bottom left) with 45 latent dimensions in the auto-encoder.

Visual inspection of the UMAPs suggests comparable performance of all four methods in separating the major cell types.

10000

[

]
.

[

]
, the V-measure

]
, the adjusted Rand index (ARI)
[

]

[

]
) are given in Section

.
These metrics further confirmed that the performance of OrchAMP matched state of the art, while OrchAMP uniquely enjoys statistical optimality guarantees and theoretically justifiable prediction set coverage in downstream querying tasks.

Appendix C
Definitions of the clustering metrics

To assess the quality of the constructed atlases in terms of separating cells of different
states
into well-defined clusters, we have employed four metrics, namely average silhouette score
[
46
]
, adjusted Rand index
[
25
]
, V-measure
[
45
]
, and cell-type LISI
[
29
]
. In this subsection, we provide brief descriptions of these clustering metrics.

1.

Average Silhouette score.

This metric
measures how well a point lies within its assigned cluster compared to other clusters. If we define
a(i)
to be the average intra-cluster Euclidean distance of the point
i
and
b(i)
to be the minimum average Euclidean distance of the point
i
to the other clusters, then the silhouette score for point
i
is given by

s(i)=\frac{b(i)-a(i)}{\max\{a(i),b(i)\}}.

In this paper, we report the average silhouette score for all points computed using their UMAP embeddings,
with cluster labels
being the ground truth
cell types.

2.

Adjusted Rand Index.

This is a measure of how well two cluster partitions of a point set align. The reconstructed atlas shows some segregation of the points into clusters, which we recovered by Louvain clustering on the atlas points. Next, let
c
be the number of point pairs assigned to the same cluster by both the cluster labels obtained by clustering the atlas points and those specified in the cell-type metadata. Similarly,
d
be the pairs of points assigned to different clusters by the two clustering schemes. Then the Rand Index (
\mathrm{RI}
) is given by

\mathrm{RI}=\frac{c+d}{{n\choose 2}}.

Let
\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{RI}]
be the expected value of the
\mathrm{RI}
under random clustering but fixed cluster sizes, then the Adjusted Rand Index is defined as:

\mathrm{ARI}=\frac{\mathrm{RI}-\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{RI}]}{\max(\mathrm{RI})-\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{RI}]},

where
\max(\mathrm{RI})
is the maximum possible value of the Rand Index given the cluster sizes of the two cluster partitions.

3.

V-measure.

The V-measure provides an entropy-based evaluation scheme of two sets of cluster labels. In our case, these sets are the cluster labels obtained using Louvain clustering on the atlas points and the original cell-type metadata. The V-measure computes two quantities, namely, homogeneity
(h)
and completeness
(c)
. Let
K
be the true cell labels and
C
be the Louvain clusters. Then

h=1-\frac{H(K\mid C)}{H(K)},\quad\mbox{and}\quad c=1-\frac{H(C\mid K)}{H(C)},

where
H(\cdot)
refers to the Shannon entropy of a distribution. Then, the V-measure is defined as

\text{V-measure}=\frac{2\times h\times c}{h+c}.

Higher values of V-measure indicate that the reconstructed atlas adheres to the cluster structure encoded in the metadata.

4.

cLISI.

The cell-type Local Inverse Simpson’s Index (cLISI) is used to characterize how homogenous the local neighborhood of each cell is when such neighborhoods are determined by the atlas embeddings. In that direction, we computed the true cell-type composition
of the local neighborhood of each cell (10 nearest neighbors). Let
p_{\ell,i}
be the proportion of cells of type
\ell
in the neighborhood of cell
i
. Then
\mathrm{LISI}_{i}
is defined as

\mathrm{LISI}_{i}=\frac{1}{\sum_{\ell}p^{2}_{\ell,i}}.

We report the average of the
\mathrm{LISI}_{i}
over all cells as the cLISI measure.

Appendix D
Proof of Proposition
5.1

Let us denote the set of true latent factors by

\mathscr{S}_{m,\widetilde{m}}=\{\bm{U}_{1},\ldots,\bm{U}_{m},\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{\widetilde{m}},\bm{V}_{1},\ldots,\bm{V}_{m}\}.

Observe that since
\frac{|\mathcal{F}_{L}|}{N}\rightarrow\lambda_{L}\in(0,1]
, using the definition of pseudo-Lipschitz functions (
5.6
) and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we can get a constant
C>0
such that for sufficiently large values of
N
,

\displaystyle\Bigg|\frac{1}{|\mathcal{F}_{L}|}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{F}_{L}}\psi(({\bm{U}}^{\mathrm{pc}}_{0,1})_{i*},\dots,({\bm{U}}^{\mathrm{pc}}_{0,m})_{i*},(\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{1})_{i*},\dots,(\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{\widetilde{m}})_{i*},(\bm{U}_{1})_{i*},\ldots,(\bm{U}_{m})_{i*},

\displaystyle\hskip 250.00038pt(\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{1})_{i*},\ldots,(\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{\widetilde{m}})_{i*})

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{|\mathcal{F}_{L}|}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{F}_{L}}\psi\bigg(\bm{S}^{L,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,1}(\bm{U}_{1})_{i*}+(\bm{\Sigma}^{L,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,1})^{1/2}(\bm{Z}^{L}_{1})_{i*},\dots,\bm{S}^{L,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,m}(\bm{U}_{m})_{i*}+(\bm{\Sigma}^{L,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,m})^{1/2}(\bm{Z}^{L}_{m})_{i*},

\displaystyle\hskip 120.00018pt(\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{1})_{i*},\dots,(\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{\widetilde{m}})_{i*},(\bm{U}_{1})_{i*},\ldots,(\bm{U}_{m})_{i*},(\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{1})_{i*},\ldots,(\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{\widetilde{m}})_{i*}\bigg)\Bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\lambda_{L}}\Bigg(1+\sum_{h=1}^{m}\frac{\|\bm{U}_{h}\|_{F}}{\sqrt{N}}+\sum_{h=1}^{m}\frac{\|{\bm{U}}^{\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}\|_{F}}{\sqrt{N}}+\sum_{h=1}^{m}\frac{\|\bm{U}_{h}(\bm{S}^{L,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h})^{\top}+\bm{Z}^{L}_{h}(\bm{\Sigma}^{L,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h})^{1/2}\|_{F}}{\sqrt{N}}

\displaystyle\hskip 250.00038pt+\sum_{\ell=1}^{\widetilde{m}}\frac{\|\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{\ell}\|_{F}}{\sqrt{N}}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{\widetilde{m}}\frac{\|\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{\ell}\|_{F}}{\sqrt{N}}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt\times\left(\sum_{h=1}^{m}\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\|{\bm{U}}^{\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}-\bm{U}_{h}(\bm{S}^{L,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h})^{\top}-\bm{Z}^{L}_{h}(\bm{\Sigma}^{L,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h})^{1/2}\|_{F}\right).

In the above asymptotic statements,
conditional on

\mathscr{S}_{m,\widetilde{m}}
,
the random matrices
\{\bm{Z}^{L}_{h}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times r_{h}}:h\in[m]\}
and
\{\bm{Z}^{R}_{h}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{h}\times r_{h}}:h\in[m]\}
are mutually independent with iid
N(0,1)
entries.
Therefore, to prove (
5.7
), it suffices to show that for all
h\in[m]
:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\left\|{\bm{U}}^{\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}-\bm{U}_{h}(\bm{S}^{L,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h})^{\top}-\bm{Z}^{L}_{h}(\bm{\Sigma}^{L,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h})^{1/2}\right\|^{2}_{F}\xrightarrow{a.s.}0.

(D.1)

The rest of the proof follows using the Strong Law of Large Numbers. Similarly, since
\frac{|\mathcal{F}_{R,h}|}{p_{h}}\rightarrow\lambda_{R,h}\in(0,1]
for all
h\in[m]
, to show (
5.8
), it suffices to show that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p_{h}}\left\|{\bm{V}}^{\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}-\bm{V}_{h}(\bm{S}^{R,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h})^{\top}-\bm{Z}^{R}_{h}(\bm{\Sigma}^{R,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h})^{1/2}\right\|^{2}_{F}\xrightarrow{a.s.}0,\quad\mbox{for all $h\in[m]$.}

(D.2)

In what follows,
we focus on proving (
D.1
).
The proof of (
D.2
) is similar.

First, let us condition on the set of true signal matrices given by
\mathscr{S}_{m,\widetilde{m}}
. Then the matrices
\widebar{\bm{X}}_{1},\ldots,\widebar{\bm{X}}_{m}
are independent. Let
\bm{U}^{\mathsf{gs}}_{1},\ldots,\bm{U}^{\mathsf{gs}}_{m}
, be the matrices obtained by applying Gram-Schmidt orthogonalization to
\bm{U}_{1},\ldots,\bm{U}_{m}
, respectively, with column norms set at
\sqrt{N}
.
For each
h\in[m]
, let the projection matrix onto the column space of
\bm{U}_{h}
and that onto the orthogonal complement of the column space of
\bm{U}_{h}
be denoted by
\bm{P}^{\parallel}_{h}
and
\bm{P}^{\perp}_{h}
, respectively. Then, by the definition of projection matrices we have

\bm{P}^{\parallel}_{h}=\frac{\bm{U}^{\mathsf{gs}}_{h}(\bm{U}^{\mathsf{gs}}_{h})^{\top}}{N}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\bm{P}^{\perp}_{h}=\bm{I}_{N}-\frac{\bm{U}^{\mathsf{gs}}_{h}(\bm{U}^{\mathsf{gs}}_{h})^{\top}}{N}.

Since, by assumption we have
(\bm{D}_{h})_{kk}>\gamma^{-1/4}_{h}
for all
k\in[r_{h}]
, using Lemma A.3 of
[
59
]
and Theorem 2.9 of
[
6
]
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{N}(\bm{U}^{\mathsf{gs}}_{h})^{\top}{\bm{U}}^{\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}=\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{N}\bm{U}^{\top}_{h}{\bm{U}}^{\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}\overset{a.s.}{=}\bm{S}^{L,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h},\quad\mbox{for all $h\in[m]$.}

(D.3)

Let us fix a set
\mathcal{T}
such that on
\mathcal{T}
, (
D.3
) holds and
\mathbb{P}_{\mu}(\mathcal{T})=1
.
Henceforth, we shall work on
\mathcal{T}
. Since,
\frac{1}{N}({\bm{U}}^{\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h})^{\top}{\bm{U}}^{\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}=\bm{I}_{N}
, we can conclude using (
D.3
) that

\frac{1}{N}(\bm{P}^{\perp}_{h}{\bm{U}}^{\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h})^{\top}(\bm{P}^{\perp}_{h}{\bm{U}}^{\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h})=\bm{I}_{N}-\bm{S}^{L,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}(\bm{S}^{L,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h})^{\top}=\bm{\Sigma}^{L,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}.

Here, the last equality follows from the definition of
\bm{\Sigma}^{L,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}
. Since
\bm{\Sigma}^{L,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}
is positive definite for all
h\in[m]
,
\bm{P}^{\perp}{\bm{U}}^{\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}
has full column rank for all
h\in[m]
.
Let
\bm{U}^{\mathsf{gs},\perp}_{h}
be the Gram-Schmidt orthogonalization of the columns of
\bm{P}^{\perp}_{h}{\bm{U}}^{\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}
. For all
h\in[m]
, we have:

\displaystyle{\bm{U}}^{\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}=\bm{P}^{\parallel}_{h}{\bm{U}}^{\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}+\bm{P}^{\perp}_{h}{\bm{U}}^{\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}=\bm{U}^{\mathsf{gs}}_{h}\bm{\Omega}_{h}+\bm{U}^{\mathsf{gs},\perp}_{h}\bm{\Omega}^{\perp}_{h},

(D.4)

where
\bm{\Omega}_{h}=N^{-1}(\bm{U}^{\mathsf{gs}}_{h})^{\top}{\bm{U}}^{\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}
and
\bm{\Omega}^{\perp}_{h}=N^{-1}(\bm{U}^{\mathsf{gs},\perp}_{h})^{\top}{\bm{U}}^{\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}
for all
h\in[m]
.
Clearly, on
\mathcal{T}
,

\bm{\Omega}_{h}\rightarrow\bm{S}^{L,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}
and
(\bm{\Omega}^{\perp}_{h})^{\top}\bm{\Omega}^{\perp}_{h}\rightarrow\bm{\Sigma}^{L,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}
, as
N\rightarrow\infty
.
Let
\{\bm{O}_{h}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times N}:h\in[m]\}

be a collection of orthogonal matrices such that for all
h\in[m]
, we have
\bm{O}_{h}\bm{U}^{\mathsf{gs}}_{h}=\bm{U}^{\mathsf{gs}}_{h}
.
By Lemma A.3 of
[
59
]
, this implies
\bm{O}_{h}\bm{U}_{h}=\bm{U}_{h}
for all
h\in[m]
. As the conditional law of
\bm{W}_{h}
given
\mathscr{S}_{m,\widetilde{m}}
is invariant under multiplication of
\bm{O}_{h}
, we have for all
h\in[m]
:

\displaystyle\bm{O}_{h}\widebar{\bm{X}}_{h}=\frac{1}{N}\bm{O}_{h}\bm{U}_{h}\bm{D}_{h}\bm{V}^{\top}_{h}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\bm{O}_{h}\bm{W}_{h}\overset{d}{=}\widebar{\bm{X}}_{h},

(D.5)

conditioned on
\mathscr{S}_{m,\widetilde{m}}
.
Since,
{\bm{U}}^{\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}
contains the leading
r_{h}
left singular vectors of
\widebar{\bm{X}}_{h}
, this implies
\bm{O}_{h}{\bm{U}}^{\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}\overset{d}{=}{\bm{U}}^{\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}
conditioned on
\mathscr{S}_{m,\widetilde{m}}
. This in turn implies that for all
h\in[m]
, conditioned on
\mathscr{S}_{m,\widetilde{m}}
, we have
\bm{O}_{h}\bm{U}^{\mathsf{gs},\perp}_{h}\overset{d}{=}\bm{U}^{\mathsf{gs},\perp}_{h}
. Therefore, conditioned on
\mathscr{S}_{m,\widetilde{m}}
, the matrices
\bm{U}^{\mathsf{gs},\perp}_{h}
, for
h\in[m]
, are Haar uniformly distributed on the Steifel Manifolds

\mathcal{U}_{h}=\Bigg\{\bm{U}^{\mathsf{gs},\perp}_{h}:\bm{U}^{\top}_{h}\bm{U}^{\mathsf{gs},\perp}_{h}=0;\,\frac{1}{N}(\bm{U}^{\mathsf{gs},\perp}_{h})^{\top}\bm{U}^{\mathsf{gs},\perp}_{h}=\bm{I}_{r_{h}}\Bigg\},

respectively, and are independent.
Using properties of the Haar measure on the Steifel manifold, we can construct a collection of independent matrices
\{\bm{Z}^{L}_{h}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times r_{h}}:h\in[m]\}
, with the entries independently and identically distributed as
N(0,1)
conditioned on
\mathscr{S}_{m,\widetilde{m}}
, such that for all
h\in[m]
we have
\bm{U}^{\mathsf{gs},\perp}_{h}=\bm{P}^{\perp}_{h}\bm{Z}^{L}_{h}\left(\frac{(\bm{Z}^{L}_{h})^{\top}\bm{P}^{\perp}_{h}\bm{Z}^{L}_{h}}{N}\right)^{-1/2}
. Using (
D.4
), for
h\in[m]
, we have the following relation:

{\bm{U}}^{\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}=\bm{U}^{\mathsf{gs}}_{h}\bm{\Omega}_{h}+\bm{P}^{\perp}_{h}\bm{Z}^{L}_{h}\left(\frac{(\bm{Z}^{L}_{h})^{\top}\bm{P}^{\perp}_{h}\bm{Z}^{L}_{h}}{N}\right)^{-1/2}\bm{\Omega}^{\perp}_{h}.

By Strong Law of Large Numbers and the fact that
\bm{P}^{\perp}_{h}
is idempotent,
\frac{(\bm{Z}^{L}_{h})^{\top}\bm{P}^{\perp}_{h}\bm{Z}^{L}_{h}}{N}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle a.s.}}{{\rightarrow}}\bm{I}_{N}
and
(1/N)\|\bm{P}^{\perp}_{h}\bm{Z}^{L}_{h}-\bm{Z}^{L}_{h}\|^{2}_{F}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle a.s.}}{{\rightarrow}}0
, as
N\rightarrow\infty
. Hence, using Lemma A.3 of
[
59
]
,
we have

\mathbb{P}\left(\left\{\frac{1}{N}\|{\bm{U}}^{\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}-\bm{U}_{h}(\bm{S}^{L,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h})^{\top}-\bm{Z}^{L}_{h}(\bm{\Sigma}^{L,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h})^{1/2}\|^{2}_{F}\rightarrow 0\;\forall h\in[m]\right\}\cap\mathcal{T}\bigg|\mathscr{S}_{m,\widetilde{m}}\right)=1.

Since
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{T})=1
,
we can conclude that

\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{1}{N}\|{\bm{U}}^{\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}-\bm{U}_{h}(\bm{S}^{L,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h})^{\top}-\bm{Z}^{L}_{h}(\bm{\Sigma}^{L,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h})^{1/2}\|^{2}_{F}\rightarrow 0\;\forall h\in[m]\right)=1.

This completes the proof of (
D.1
).

Appendix E
Consistency of nuisance parameter estimation

E.1
Proof of Lemma
5.1

The first assertion follows using Assumption
5.1
(5) and
[
6
, Theorem 2.9]
. Since
\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[(\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{\ell})_{i*}(\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{\ell})^{\top}_{i*}]=\bm{I}_{{\widetilde{r}}_{\ell}}
for all
\ell\in[\widetilde{m}]
and
i\in[N]
, and for all
\ell\in[\widetilde{m}]
the matrices
\bm{L}_{\ell}
are symmetric; the second assertion can be shown using the Strong Law of Large Numbers. Finally, the third assertion follows using the first assertion and Theorem
5.1
.

E.2
Proof of Lemma
5.2

Consider
\{\widehat{\bm{S}}^{L,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h},\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}^{L,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h},\widehat{\bm{S}}^{R,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h},\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}^{R,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}:h\in[m]\}
and
\{\widehat{\bm{L}}_{\ell}:\ell\in[\widetilde{m}]\}
defined in (
2.9
) and (
2.10
), respectively. For some sequences of priors
\{\mu_{N}\}\subseteq\mathcal{P}
and
\{\nu_{h,N}\in\mathcal{P}_{\nu_{h}}:h\in[m],N\in\mathbb{N}\}
, let us define
the functions
\{f^{L}_{N}\}
and
\{f^{R}_{h,N}:h\in[m]\}
as follows:

\displaystyle f^{L}_{N}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m},\widetilde{x}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{x}_{\widetilde{m}};\mu_{N})

(E.1)

\displaystyle:=\int_{u,\tilde{u}}\prod_{h=1}^{m}\exp\left(-\frac{[x_{h}-\widehat{\bm{S}}^{L,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}u_{h}]^{\top}(\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}^{L,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h})^{-1}[x_{h}-\widehat{\bm{S}}^{L,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}u_{h}]}{2}\right)\times

(E.2)

\displaystyle\hskip 45.00006pt\prod_{\ell=1}^{\widetilde{m}}\exp\left(-\frac{[\widetilde{x}_{\ell}-\widehat{\bm{L}}_{\ell}\widetilde{u}_{\ell}]^{\top}[\widetilde{x}_{\ell}-\widehat{\bm{L}}_{\ell}\widetilde{u}_{\ell}]}{2}\right)\,\times

(E.3)

\displaystyle\hskip 85.00012ptd\mu_{N}(u_{1},\ldots,u_{m},\widetilde{u}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{u}_{\widetilde{m}}),

(E.4)

and

f^{R}_{h,N}(y_{h};\nu_{h,N}):=\int_{v_{h}}\exp\left(-\frac{[y_{h}-\widehat{\bm{S}}^{R,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}v_{h}]^{\top}(\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}^{R,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h})^{-1}[y_{h}-\widehat{\bm{S}}^{R,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}v_{h}]}{2}\right)d\nu_{h,N}(v_{h}).

Similarly, define the functions
f^{L}
and
\{f^{R}_{h}:h\in[m]\}
as follows:

\displaystyle f^{L}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m},\widetilde{x}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{x}_{\widetilde{m}};\mu)

(E.5)

\displaystyle:=\int_{u,\tilde{u}}\prod_{h=1}^{m}\exp\left(-\frac{[x_{h}-\bm{S}^{L,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}u_{h}]^{\top}(\bm{\Sigma}^{L,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h})^{-1}[x_{h}-\bm{S}^{L,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}u_{h}]}{2}\right)\times

(E.6)

\displaystyle\hskip 45.00006pt\prod_{\ell=1}^{\widetilde{m}}\exp\left(-\frac{[\widetilde{x}_{\ell}-\bm{L}_{\ell}\widetilde{u}_{\ell}]^{\top}[\widetilde{x}_{\ell}-\bm{L}_{\ell}\widetilde{u}_{\ell}]}{2}\right)\,\times

(E.7)

\displaystyle\hskip 85.00012ptd\mu(u_{1},\ldots,u_{m},\widetilde{u}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{u}_{\widetilde{m}}),

(E.8)

and

f^{R}_{h}(y_{h};\nu_{h}):=\int_{v_{h}}\exp\left(-\frac{[y_{h}-\bm{S}^{R,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}v_{h}]^{\top}(\bm{\Sigma}^{R,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h})^{-1}[y_{h}-\bm{S}^{R,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}v_{h}]}{2}\right)d\nu_{h}(v_{h}).

Lemma E.1
.

If the sequence of estimators
\{\mu_{N}\}
and
\{\nu_{h,N}:h\in[m],N\in\mathbb{N}\}
are approximately maximum likelihood estimators of the true priors
\mu
and
\{\nu_{h}:h\in[m]\}
, respectively, i.e., they satisfy the following relations

\displaystyle\liminf_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\log\frac{f^{L}_{N}(({\bm{U}}^{\mathrm{pc}}_{0,1})_{i*},\ldots,({\bm{U}}^{\mathrm{pc}}_{0,m})_{i*},(\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{1})_{i*},\ldots,(\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{\widetilde{m}})_{i*};\mu_{N})}{f^{L}(({\bm{U}}^{\mathrm{pc}}_{0,1})_{i*},\ldots,({\bm{U}}^{\mathrm{pc}}_{0,m})_{i*},(\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{1})_{i*},\ldots,(\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{\widetilde{m}})_{i*};\mu)}\geq 0,

(E.9)

and

\displaystyle\liminf_{p_{h}\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{p_{h}}\sum_{j=1}^{p_{h}}\log\frac{f^{R}_{h,N}(({\bm{V}}^{\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h})_{j*};\nu_{h,N})}{f^{R}_{h}(({\bm{V}}^{\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h})_{j*};\nu_{h})}\geq 0\quad\mbox{for $h\in[m]$;}

(E.10)

then as
N,p_{h}\rightarrow\infty
, we have
\mu_{N}\overset{w}{\rightarrow}\mu
and
\nu_{h,N}\overset{w}{\rightarrow}\nu_{h}
for all
h\in[m]
, almost surely.

The proof of this theorem follows by the arguments presented in Lemma B.2 of
Zhong et al., [59]
and the consistency of
\widehat{\bm{S}}^{L,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h},\widehat{\bm{S}}^{R,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h},\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}^{L,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h},\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}^{R,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}
and
\widehat{\bm{L}}_{\ell}
in estimating
\bm{S}^{L,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h},\bm{S}^{R,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h},\bm{\Sigma}^{L,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h},\bm{\Sigma}^{R,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}
and
\bm{L}_{\ell}
, respectively, for all
h\in[m]
and
\ell\in[\widetilde{m}]
(Lemma
5.1
). Hence, we omit the details. Now, consider
\widehat{\mu}
and
\widehat{\nu}_{h}
defined by (
2.13
) and (
2.15
).

Proof of Lemma
5.2
.

The proof of Lemma
5.2
follows from Lemma
E.1
, the techniques used to prove Corollary B.7 of
[
59
]
.
∎

Appendix F
Asymptotics of the oracle AMP

To analyze the asymptotics of the AMP iterates with the denoisers constructed using the estimated priors
\widehat{\mu}
and
\widehat{\nu}_{h}
, we first analyze the asymptotics of the Oracle AMP iterates defined in (
5.13
). Then we show that as
N\rightarrow\infty
, the differences between the two sets of iterates converge to zero.

In that direction, let us consider the following theorem:

Theorem F.1
.

Consider the iterates
\{{\bm{U}}^{\circ}_{t,h},{\bm{V}}^{\circ}_{t,h}:t\geq 0,h\in[m]\}
given by (
5.13
). Then, for any sequence of deterministic subsets
\mathcal{F}_{L}\subset[N]
satisfying
\frac{|\mathcal{F}_{L}|}{N}\rightarrow\lambda_{L}\in(0,1]
, as
N\rightarrow\infty
and any
pseudo-Lipschitz
function
\psi:\mathbb{R}^{2(r+\widetilde{r})}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
, we have the following:

\displaystyle\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{|\mathcal{F}_{L}|}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{F}_{L}}\psi(({\bm{U}}^{\circ}_{t,1})_{i*},\ldots,({\bm{U}}^{\circ}_{t,m})_{i*},(\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{1})_{i*},\ldots,(\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{\widetilde{m}})_{i*},(\bm{U}_{1})_{i*},\ldots,(\bm{U}_{m})_{i*},

(F.1)

\displaystyle\hskip 250.00038pt(\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{1})_{i*},\ldots,(\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{\widetilde{m}})_{i*})

(F.2)

\displaystyle\hskip 40.00006pt\overset{a.s.}{=}\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\left[\psi(Y^{\circ}_{t,1},\ldots,Y^{\circ}_{t,m},\widetilde{Y}_{0,1},\ldots,\widetilde{Y}_{0,\widetilde{m}},U_{1},\ldots,U_{m},\tilde{U}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{U}_{\widetilde{m}})\right].

(F.3)

where
\{Y^{\circ}_{t,h}:h\in[m]\}
and
\{\widetilde{Y}_{0,\ell}:\ell\in[\widetilde{m}]\}
are defined by (
5.10
) and the collection of random vectors
(U_{1},\ldots,U_{m},\widetilde{U}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{U}_{\widetilde{m}})\sim\mu
.
Next, for all
h\in[m]
, if we consider any sequence of deterministic subsets
\mathcal{F}_{R,h}\subset[p_{h}]
that satisfy
\frac{|\mathcal{F}_{R,h}|}{p_{h}}\rightarrow\lambda_{R,h}\in(0,1]
as
p_{h}\rightarrow\infty
and
pseudo-Lipschitz
functions
\varphi_{h}:\mathbb{R}^{2r_{h}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
, we have the following:

\displaystyle\lim_{p_{h}\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{|\mathcal{F}_{R,h}|}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{F}_{R,h}}\varphi_{h}(({\bm{V}}^{\circ}_{t,h})_{i*},(\bm{V}_{h})_{i*})\overset{a.s.}{=}\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{h}}\left[\varphi_{h}(Y^{R,\circ}_{t,h},V_{h})\right],

(F.4)

where
\{Y^{R,\circ}_{t,h}:h\in[m]\}
are defined by (
5.11
) and
V_{h}\sim\nu_{h}
.

Let
\bm{P}^{\perp}_{{\bm{U}}^{\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}}
and
\bm{P}^{\perp}_{{\bm{V}}^{\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}}
be the projection matrices onto the orthogonal complements of
{\bm{U}}^{\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}
and
{\bm{V}}^{\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}
. For all
h\in[m]
, let us consider the matrices
\bm{X}^{\perp}_{h}
defined as follows:

\displaystyle\bm{X}^{\perp}_{h}=\frac{1}{N}{\bm{U}}^{\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}\bm{D}_{0,h}({\bm{V}}^{\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h})^{\top}+\bm{P}^{\perp}_{{\bm{U}}^{\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}}\left(\frac{1}{N}\bm{U}_{h}\bm{D}_{h}\bm{V}_{h}^{\top}+\bm{W}^{\perp}_{h}\right)\bm{P}^{\perp}_{{\bm{V}}^{\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}},

(F.5)

where
\bm{W}^{\perp}_{h}\overset{d}{=}\bm{W}_{h}/\sqrt{N}
and is independent of
\bm{X}_{1},\ldots,\bm{X}_{m},\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{\widetilde{m}}
.
Using the techniques from Lemma B.3 of
Montanari and Venkataramanan, [39]
and
Benaych-Georges and Nadakuditi, [6]
, we can show that there exists a constant
\varepsilon_{0}>0
such that for any
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0})
, if the singular value matrices satisfy

\displaystyle\max_{k\in[r_{h}]}\left|(\bm{D}_{0,h})_{kk}-\sqrt{\frac{\left(\gamma_{h}(\bm{D}_{h})^{2}_{kk}+1\right)\left((\bm{D}_{h})^{2}_{kk}+1\right)}{\gamma_{h}(\bm{D}_{h})^{2}_{kk}}}\right|\leq\varepsilon,\quad\text{for all }h\in[m],

(F.6)

and the left singular vectors satisfy

\displaystyle\min_{k\in[r_{h}]}\left\{\frac{1}{N}\left|({\bm{U}}^{\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h})^{\top}_{*k}(\bm{U}_{h})_{*k}\right|^{2}-(s^{L}_{0,h})_{k}\right\}\geq-\varepsilon,\quad\text{for all }h\in[m],

(F.7)

and the right singular vectors satisfy

\displaystyle\min_{k\in[r_{h}]}\left\{\frac{1}{p_{h}}\left|({\bm{V}}^{\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h})^{\top}_{*k}(\bm{V}_{h})_{*k}\right|^{2}-(s^{R}_{0,h})_{k}\right\}\geq-\varepsilon,\quad\text{for all }h\in[m],

(F.8)

for
(s^{L}_{0,h},s^{R}_{0,h})
defined in (
2.7
), then the following coupling bound holds:

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbb{P}\left(\{\bm{X}_{h}\}_{h=1}^{m},\{\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{\ell}\}_{\ell=1}^{\widetilde{m}}\in\cdot\;\middle|\;\{\bm{D}_{0,h},{\bm{U}}^{\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h},{\bm{V}}^{\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}\}_{h=1}^{m}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 40.00006pt-\left.\mathbb{P}\left(\{\bm{X}^{\perp}_{h}\}_{h=1}^{m},\{\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{\ell}\}_{\ell=1}^{\widetilde{m}}\in\cdot\;\middle|\;\{\bm{D}_{0,h},{\bm{U}}^{\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h},{\bm{V}}^{\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}\}_{h=1}^{m}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{TV}}

\displaystyle\hskip 180.00027pt\leq\frac{1}{c(\varepsilon)}e^{-Nc(\varepsilon)},

(F.9)

with probability at least
1-e^{-Nc(\varepsilon)}
, where
c(\varepsilon)>0
is a constant depending only on
\varepsilon
. Fix
\varepsilon\in[\varepsilon_{0}/2,\varepsilon_{0}]
. Using (2.1) of
[
59
]
and Proposition 5.1, we can get a
N_{0}(\varepsilon)>0
such that (
F.6
), (
F.7
) and (
F.8
) holds for all
N\geq N_{0}(\varepsilon)
. Let us consider the following modified AMP iterations:

\displaystyle\widebar{\bm{V}}^{\perp}_{t,h}=v^{\circ}_{t,h}(\bm{V}^{\perp}_{t,h};\nu_{h}),

(F.10)

\displaystyle\bm{U}^{\perp}_{t,h}=\bm{X}^{\perp}_{h}\widebar{\bm{V}}^{\perp}_{t,h}-\widebar{\bm{U}}^{\perp}_{t-1,h}\gamma_{h}\left(\bm{J}^{R,\circ}_{t,h}(\bm{V}^{\perp}_{t,h};\nu_{h})\right)^{\top}

(F.11)

\displaystyle\widebar{\bm{U}}^{\perp}_{t,h}=u^{\circ}_{t,h}(\widebar{\bm{U}}^{\perp}_{t,1},\ldots,\widebar{\bm{U}}^{\perp}_{t,m},\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{\widetilde{m}};\mu),

(F.12)

\displaystyle\bm{V}^{\perp}_{t+1,h}=(\bm{X}^{\perp}_{h})^{\top}\widebar{\bm{U}}^{\perp}_{t,h}-\widebar{\bm{V}}^{\perp}_{t,h}\left(\bm{J}^{L,\circ}_{t,h}(\widebar{\bm{U}}^{\perp}_{t,1},\ldots,\widebar{\bm{U}}^{\perp}_{t,m},\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{\widetilde{m}};\mu)\right)^{\top},

(F.13)

with
\bm{V}^{\perp}_{0,h}={\bm{V}}^{\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}
,
\bm{U}^{\perp}_{-1,h}={\bm{U}}^{\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}(\bm{\Sigma}^{L,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h})^{1/2}
and the same set of state-evolution recursions given by (
5.9
). In addition, the matrix-valued functions
\{\bm{J}^{R,\circ}_{t,h},\bm{J}^{L,\circ}_{t,h}:t\geq 0,h\in[m]\}
are defined in (
5.14
) and (
5.15
) respectively. Then, using steps similar to Eq.(A.10)– Eq.(A.11) of
[
39
]
and (
F.9
), we can show that there exists a coupling of the laws of
\{\bm{X}_{h}\}_{h=1}^{m},\{\bm{X}^{\perp}_{h}\}_{h=1}^{m}
and
\{\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{\ell}\}_{\ell=1}^{\widetilde{m}}
such that for any
pseudo-Lipschitz
function
\psi:\mathbb{R}^{2(r+\widetilde{r})}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg[\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\psi((\bm{U}^{\perp}_{t,1})_{i*},\dots,(\bm{U}^{\perp}_{t,m})_{i*},(\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{1})_{i*},\dots,(\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{\widetilde{m}})_{i*},

(F.14)

\displaystyle\hskip 150.00023pt(\bm{U}_{1})_{i*},\ldots,(\bm{U}_{m})_{i*},(\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{1})_{i*},\ldots,(\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{\widetilde{m}})_{i*})

(F.15)

\displaystyle\hskip 15.00002pt\neq\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\psi((\bm{U}^{\circ}_{t,1})_{i*},\dots,(\bm{U}^{\circ}_{t,1})_{i*},(\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{1})_{i*},\dots,(\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{\widetilde{m}})_{i*},

(F.16)

\displaystyle\hskip 150.00023pt(\bm{U}_{1})_{i*},\ldots,(\bm{U}_{m})_{i*},(\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{1})_{i*},\ldots,(\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{\widetilde{m}})_{i*})\bigg]

(F.17)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{c(\varepsilon)}e^{-Nc(\varepsilon)},\quad\mbox{for $c(\varepsilon)>0$ defined in \eqref{eq:coupling} and for all $N\geq N_{0}(\varepsilon)$.}

(F.18)

Furthermore, for any
pseudo-Lipschitz
functions
\varphi_{h}:\mathbb{R}^{2r_{h}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Bigg[\frac{1}{p_{h}}\sum_{i=1}^{p_{h}}\varphi_{h}((\bm{V}^{\perp}_{t,h})_{i*},(\bm{V}_{h})_{i*})\neq\frac{1}{p_{h}}\sum_{i=1}^{p_{h}}\varphi_{h}((\bm{V}^{\circ}_{t,h})_{i*},(\bm{V}_{h})_{i*})\Bigg]

(F.19)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{c(\varepsilon)}e^{-Nc(\varepsilon)},\quad\mbox{for all $p_{h}\geq\lceil N_{0}(\varepsilon)\gamma_{h}\rceil$.}

(F.20)

Since
p_{h}/N\rightarrow\gamma_{h}
for all
h\in[m]
, using the Borel Cantelli Lemmas, the above displays imply

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\psi((\bm{U}^{\perp}_{t,1})_{i*},\dots,(\bm{U}^{\perp}_{t,m})_{i*},(\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{1})_{i*},\dots,(\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{\widetilde{m}})_{i*},(\bm{U}_{1})_{i*},\ldots,(\bm{U}_{m})_{i*},

\displaystyle\hskip 150.00023pt(\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{1})_{i*},\ldots,(\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{\widetilde{m}})_{i*})

(F.21)

\displaystyle\overset{a.s.}{=}\lim\limits_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\psi((\bm{U}^{\circ}_{t,1})_{i*},\dots,(\bm{U}^{\circ}_{t,1})_{i*},(\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{1})_{i*},\dots,(\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{\widetilde{m}})_{i*},

(F.22)

\displaystyle\hskip 150.00023pt(\bm{U}_{1})_{i*},\ldots,(\bm{U}_{m})_{i*},(\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{1})_{i*},\ldots,(\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{\widetilde{m}})_{i*}),

(F.23)

and

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{p_{h}\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{p_{h}}\sum_{i=1}^{p_{h}}\varphi_{h}((\bm{V}^{\perp}_{t,h})_{i*},(\bm{V}_{h})_{i*})\overset{a.s.}{=}\lim\limits_{p_{h}\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{p_{h}}\sum_{i=1}^{p_{h}}\varphi((\bm{V}^{\circ}_{t,h})_{i*},(\bm{V}_{h})_{i*}).

Since,
|\mathcal{F}_{L}|/N\rightarrow\lambda_{L}
and
|\mathcal{F}_{R,h}|/p_{h}\rightarrow\lambda_{R,h}
for all
h\in[m]
, therefore one can also conclude that

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{|\mathcal{F}_{L}|}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{F}_{L}}\psi((\bm{U}^{\perp}_{t,1})_{i*},\dots,(\bm{U}^{\perp}_{t,m})_{i*},(\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{1})_{i*},\dots,(\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{\widetilde{m}})_{i*},(\bm{U}_{1})_{i*},\ldots,(\bm{U}_{m})_{i*},

\displaystyle\hskip 150.00023pt(\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{1})_{i*},\ldots,(\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{\widetilde{m}})_{i*})

(F.25)

\displaystyle\overset{a.s.}{=}\lim\limits_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{|\mathcal{F}_{L}|}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{F}_{L}}\psi((\bm{U}^{\circ}_{t,1})_{i*},\dots,(\bm{U}^{\circ}_{t,1})_{i*},(\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{1})_{i*},\dots,(\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{\widetilde{m}})_{i*},

(F.26)

\displaystyle\hskip 150.00023pt(\bm{U}_{1})_{i*},\ldots,(\bm{U}_{m})_{i*},(\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{1})_{i*},\ldots,(\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{\widetilde{m}})_{i*}),

(F.27)

and

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{p_{h}\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{|\mathcal{F}_{R,h}|}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{F}_{R,h}}\varphi_{h}((\bm{V}^{\perp}_{t,h})_{i*},(\bm{V}_{h})_{i*})\overset{a.s.}{=}\lim\limits_{p_{h}\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{|\mathcal{F}_{R,h}|}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{F}_{R,h}}\varphi((\bm{V}^{\circ}_{t,h})_{i*},(\bm{V}_{h})_{i*}).

Hence, it is enough to analyze the AMP iterations given by (
F.10
).

F.1
Proof of Theorem
F.1

Due to the above-mentioned arguments, we shall analyze the AMP iterations given by (
F.10
). In that direction, for all
t\geq 0
, let us consider random vectors
\{Y^{L,\perp}_{t,h},Y^{R,\perp}_{t,h}\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{h}}:h\in[m]\}
generated as follows:

\displaystyle Y^{L,\perp}_{t,h}

\displaystyle\sim N_{r_{h}}\bigg(\bm{A}^{L,\perp}_{t,h}U_{h}+\bm{B}^{L,\perp}_{t,h}Y_{0,h},\bm{T}^{L,\perp}_{t,h}\bigg)

(F.29)

\displaystyle Y^{R,\perp}_{t,h}

\displaystyle\sim N_{r_{h}}\bigg(\bm{A}^{R,\perp}_{t,h}V_{h}+\bm{B}^{R,\perp}_{t,h}Y^{R}_{0,h},\bm{T}^{R,\perp}_{t,h}\bigg),

(F.30)

In the above equation,
(U_{1},\ldots,U_{m},\widetilde{U}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{U}_{\widetilde{m}})\sim\mu
,
V_{h}\sim\nu_{h}
for all
h\in[m]
and
\{Y_{0,h},Y^{R}_{0,h}:h\in[m]\}
are defined in (
2.11
) and (
2.14
) respectively. Furthermore, for all
h\in[m]
, the matrices

\bm{A}^{L,\perp}_{t,h},\bm{B}^{L,\perp}_{t,h},\bm{T}^{L,\perp}_{t,h},\bm{A}^{R,\perp}_{t,h},\bm{B}^{R,\perp}_{t,h},\bm{T}^{R,\perp}_{t,h}\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{h}\times r_{h}}

are recursively defined as follows:

\displaystyle\bm{A}^{R,\perp}_{0,h}=\bm{0},\quad\bm{B}^{R,\perp}_{0,h}=\bm{I}_{r_{h}},\quad\bm{T}^{R,\perp}_{0,h}=\bm{0},\quad\bm{A}^{L,\perp}_{-1,h}=\bm{0},\quad\bm{B}^{L,\perp}_{-1,h}=(\bm{\Sigma}^{L,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h})^{1/2},\quad\bm{T}^{L,\perp}_{-1,h}=\bm{0},

(F.31)

\displaystyle\bm{A}^{L,\perp}_{t,h}

\displaystyle=\gamma_{h}\bigg\{\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{h}}[v^{\circ}_{t,h}(Y^{R,\perp}_{t,h};\nu_{h})V^{\top}_{h}]\bm{D}_{h}-\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{h}}[v^{\circ}_{t,h}(Y^{R,\perp}_{t,h};\nu_{h})(Y^{R}_{0,h})^{\top}]\bm{S}^{R,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}\bm{D}_{h}\bigg\},

(F.32)

\displaystyle\bm{B}^{L,\perp}_{t,h}

\displaystyle=\gamma_{h}\bigg\{\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{h}}[v^{\circ}_{t,h}(Y^{R,\perp}_{t,h};\nu_{h})(Y^{R}_{0,h})^{\top}]\bm{D}^{\ast}_{h}-\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{h}}[v^{\circ}_{t,h}(Y^{R,\perp}_{t,h};\nu_{h})V^{\top}_{h}]\bm{D}_{h}(\bm{S}^{L,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h})^{\top}

(F.33)

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{h}}[v^{\circ}_{t,h}(Y^{R,\perp}_{t,h};\nu_{h})(Y^{R}_{0,h})^{\top}]\bm{S}^{R,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}\bm{D}_{h}(\bm{S}^{L,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h})^{\top}

(F.34)

\displaystyle\qquad-\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{h}}[\mathsf{d}v^{\circ}_{t,h}(Y^{R,\perp}_{t,h};\nu_{h})]\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[u^{\circ}_{t-1,h}(Y^{L,\perp}_{t-1,h},\ldots,Y^{L,\perp}_{t-1,m},\widetilde{Y}_{0,1},\ldots,\widetilde{Y}_{0,\widetilde{m}})(Y_{0,h})^{\top}]\bigg\},

(F.35)

\displaystyle\bm{T}^{L,\perp}_{t,h}

\displaystyle=\gamma_{h}\bigg\{\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{h}}[v^{\circ}_{t,h}(Y^{R,\perp}_{t,h};\nu_{h})v^{\circ}_{t,h}(Y^{R,\perp}_{t,h};\nu_{h})^{\top}]

(F.36)

\displaystyle\qquad-\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{h}}[v_{t,h}(Y^{R,\perp}_{t,h};\nu_{h})(Y^{R}_{0,h})^{\top}]\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{h}}[(Y^{R}_{0,h})v_{t,h}(Y^{R,\perp}_{t,h};\nu_{h})^{\top}]\bigg\},

(F.37)

\displaystyle\bm{A}^{R,\perp}_{t+1,h}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[u^{\circ}_{t,h}(Y^{L,\perp}_{t,1},\ldots,Y^{L,\perp}_{t,m},\widetilde{Y}_{0,1},\ldots,\widetilde{Y}_{0,\widetilde{m}};\mu)U^{\top}_{h}]\bm{D}_{h}

(F.38)

\displaystyle\qquad-\mathbb{E}[u^{\circ}_{t,h}(Y^{L,\perp}_{t,1},\ldots,Y^{L,\perp}_{t,m},\widetilde{Y}_{0,1},\ldots,\widetilde{Y}_{0,\widetilde{m}};\mu)(Y_{0,h})^{\top}]\bm{S}^{L,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}\bm{D}_{h},

(F.39)

\displaystyle\bm{B}^{R,\perp}_{t+1,h}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[u^{\circ}_{t,h}(Y^{L,\perp}_{t,1},\ldots,Y^{L,\perp}_{t,m},\widetilde{Y}_{0,1},\ldots,\widetilde{Y}_{0,\widetilde{m}};\mu)Y^{\top}_{0,h}]\bm{D}^{*}_{h}

(F.40)

\displaystyle\qquad-\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[u^{\circ}_{t,h}(Y^{L,\perp}_{t,1},\ldots,Y^{L,\perp}_{t,m},\widetilde{Y}_{0,1},\ldots,\widetilde{Y}_{0,\widetilde{m}};\mu)U^{\top}_{h}]\bm{D}_{h}(\bm{S}^{R,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h})^{\top}

(F.41)

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[u^{\circ}_{t,h}(Y^{L,\perp}_{t,1},\ldots,Y^{L,\perp}_{t,m},\widetilde{Y}_{0,1},\ldots,\widetilde{Y}_{0,\widetilde{m}};\mu)Y^{\top}_{0,h}]\bm{S}^{L,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}\bm{D}_{h}(\bm{S}^{R,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h})^{\top}

(F.42)

\displaystyle\qquad-\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[\mathsf{d}_{h}u^{\circ}_{t,h}(Y^{L,\perp}_{t,1},\ldots,Y^{L,\perp}_{t,m},\widetilde{Y}_{0,1},\ldots,\widetilde{Y}_{0,\widetilde{m}};\mu)]\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{h}}[v^{\circ}_{t,h}(Y^{R,\perp}_{t,h})(Y^{R}_{0,h})^{\top}],

(F.43)

\displaystyle\bm{T}^{R,\perp}_{t+1,h}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[u^{\circ}_{t,h}(Y^{L,\perp}_{t,1},\ldots,Y^{L,\perp}_{t,m},\widetilde{Y}_{0,1},\ldots,\widetilde{Y}_{0,\widetilde{m}};\mu)^{\otimes 2}]

(F.44)

\displaystyle\qquad-\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[u^{\circ}_{t,h}(Y^{L,\perp}_{t,1},\ldots,Y^{L,\perp}_{t,m},\widetilde{Y}_{0,1},\ldots,\widetilde{Y}_{0,\widetilde{m}};\mu)Y^{\top}_{0,h}]^{\otimes 2}

(F.45)

where
\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\bm{D}_{0,h}\overset{a.s.}{=}\bm{D}^{*}_{h}
, the matrices
\{\mathsf{d}v^{\circ}_{t,h}(\cdot):h\in[m]\}
are the Jacobian matrices of the functions
\{v^{\circ}_{t,h}(\cdot):h\in[m]\}
and
\{\mathsf{d}_{h}u^{\circ}_{t,h}(\cdot):h\in[m]\}
are the Jacobian matrices of the function
\{u^{\circ}_{t,h}(\cdot):h\in[m]\}
with respect to the variables corresponding to the
h
-th modality. By convention we set

\mathbb{E}[u^{\circ}_{-1,h}(Y^{L,\perp}_{-1,1},\ldots,Y^{L,\perp}_{-1,m},\widetilde{Y}_{0,1},\ldots,\widetilde{Y}_{0,\widetilde{m}};\mu)Y^{\top}_{0,h}]=(\bm{\Sigma}^{L,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h})^{1/2}.

Next, consider the following lemmas.

Lemma F.1
.

Consider the sequence of deterministic subsets
\mathcal{F}_{L}\subset[N]
satisfying
\frac{|\mathcal{F}_{L}|}{N}\rightarrow\lambda_{L}\in(0,1]
, as
N\rightarrow\infty
and
\{\mathcal{F}_{R,h}\subset[p_{h}]:h\in[m]\}
satisfying
\frac{|\mathcal{F}_{R,h}|}{p_{h}}\rightarrow\lambda_{R,h}\in(0,1]
, as
p_{h}\rightarrow\infty
for all
h\in[m]
. Then, for any pseudo-Lipschitz functions
\psi:\mathbb{R}^{3r+2\widetilde{r}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
and
\phi_{h}:\mathbb{R}^{3r_{h}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
for
h\in[m]
, the following limits hold almost surely.

\displaystyle\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\Bigg|\frac{1}{|\mathcal{F}_{L}|}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{F}_{L}}\psi\left(\{(\bm{U}^{\perp}_{t,h})_{i*}\}_{h=1}^{m},\{({\bm{U}}^{\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h})_{i*}\}_{h=1}^{m},\{(\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{h})_{i*}\}_{h=1}^{\widetilde{m}},\{(\bm{U}_{h})_{i*}\}_{h=1}^{m},\{(\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{\ell})_{i*}\}_{\ell=1}^{\widetilde{m}}\right)

(F.46)

\displaystyle\hskip 40.00006pt-\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\left[\psi\left(\{\bm{A}^{L,\perp}_{t,h}U_{h}+\bm{B}^{L,\perp}_{t,h}Y_{0,h}+(\bm{T}^{L,\perp}_{t,h})^{1/2}W^{\perp,L}_{t,h}\}_{h=1}^{m},\{Y_{0,h}\}_{h=1}^{m},\right.\right.

(F.47)

\displaystyle\hskip 150.00023pt\left.\left.\{\widetilde{Y}_{0,\ell}\}_{\ell=1}^{\widetilde{m}},\{U_{h}\}_{h=1}^{m},\{\widetilde{U}_{\ell}\}_{\ell=1}^{\widetilde{m}}\right)\right]\Bigg|=0,

(F.48)

and

\displaystyle\lim_{p_{h}\rightarrow\infty}\Bigg|\frac{1}{|\mathcal{F}_{R,h}|}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{F}_{R,h}}\phi_{h}\left((\bm{V}^{\perp}_{t,h})_{i*},({\bm{V}}^{\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h})_{i*},(\bm{V}_{h})_{i*}\right)

(F.49)

\displaystyle\hskip 40.00006pt-\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{h}}\left[\phi_{h}\left(\bm{A}^{R,\perp}_{t,h}V_{h}+\bm{B}^{R,\perp}_{t,h}Y^{R}_{0,h}+(\bm{T}^{R,\perp}_{t,h})^{1/2}W^{\perp,R}_{t,h},Y^{R}_{0,h},V_{h}\right)\right]\Bigg|=0,

(F.50)

where for all
t\geq 0
and
h\in[m]
, the random variables
W^{\perp,L}_{t,h},W^{\perp,R}_{t,h}\sim N_{r_{h}}(0,\bm{I}_{r_{h}})
and are mutually independent. In addition,
(U_{1},\ldots,U_{m},\widetilde{U}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{U}_{\widetilde{m}})\sim\mu
and
\{V_{h}\sim\nu_{h}:h\in[m]\}
.

Lemma F.2
.

For any sequence of pseudo-Lipschitz functions
\psi:\mathbb{R}^{3r+2\widetilde{r}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
and
\phi_{h}:\mathbb{R}^{3r_{h}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
for
h\in[m]
, the following limits hold almost surely.

\displaystyle\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\Bigg|\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\left[\psi\left(\{\bm{A}^{L,\perp}_{t,h}U_{h}+\bm{B}^{L,\perp}_{t,h}Y_{0,h}+(\bm{T}^{L,\perp}_{t,h})^{1/2}W^{\perp,L}_{t,h}\}_{h=1}^{m},\{Y_{0,h}\}_{h=1}^{m},\right.\right.

(F.51)

\displaystyle\hskip 200.0003pt\left.\left.\{\widetilde{Y}_{0,\ell}\}_{\ell=1}^{\widetilde{m}},\{U_{h}\}_{h=1}^{m},\{\widetilde{U}_{\ell}\}_{\ell=1}^{\widetilde{m}}\right)\right]

(F.52)

\displaystyle\hskip 60.00009pt-\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\left[\psi\left(\{\bm{S}^{L,\circ}_{t,h}U_{h}+(\bm{\Sigma}^{L,\circ}_{t,h})^{1/2}Z^{\perp,L}_{t,h}\}_{h=1}^{m},\{Y_{0,h}\}_{h=1}^{m},\right.\right.

(F.53)

\displaystyle\hskip 200.0003pt\left.\left.\{\widetilde{Y}_{0,\ell}\}_{\ell=1}^{\widetilde{m}},\{U_{h}\}_{h=1}^{m},\{\widetilde{U}_{\ell}\}_{\ell=1}^{\widetilde{m}}\right)\right]\Bigg|=0,

(F.54)

and

\displaystyle\lim_{p_{h}\rightarrow\infty}\Bigg|\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{h}}\left[\phi_{h}\left(\bm{A}^{R,\perp}_{t,h}V_{h}+\bm{B}^{R,\perp}_{t,h}Y^{R}_{0,h}+(\bm{T}^{R,\perp}_{t,h})^{1/2}W^{\perp,R}_{t,h},Y^{R}_{0,h},V_{h}\right)\right]-

(F.55)

\displaystyle\hskip 40.00006pt\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{h}}\left[\phi_{h}\left(\bm{S}^{R,\circ}_{t,h}V_{h}+(\bm{\Sigma}^{R,\circ}_{t,h})^{1/2}Z^{\perp,R}_{t,h},Y^{R}_{0,h},V_{h}\right)\right]\Bigg|=0,

(F.56)

where for all
t\geq 0
and
h\in[m]
, the random variables
Z^{\perp,L}_{t,h},Z^{\perp,R}_{t,h}\sim N_{r_{h}}(0,\bm{I}_{r_{h}})
and are independent.

Proof of Theorem
F.1
.

Using (
F.25
) and (
F
) along with Lemmas
F.1
and
F.2
, the result follows.
∎

F.2
Proof of Lemma
F.2

Observe that by definition, as
N\rightarrow\infty

\|\bm{S}^{R,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}\bm{D}_{h}-\bm{D}_{h}\bm{S}^{R,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}\|^{2}_{F}\xrightarrow{a.s.}0,\quad\mbox{and}\quad\|\bm{S}^{L,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}\bm{D}_{h}-\bm{D}_{h}\bm{S}^{L,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}\|^{2}_{F}\xrightarrow{a.s.}0.

Using the definitions of
\bm{S}^{L,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h},\bm{S}^{R,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}
from (
2.7
) and that of
\{\bm{B}^{L,\perp}_{t,h},\bm{B}^{R,\perp}_{t,h}\}_{t\geq-1}
we get the following limits using the Stein’s Lemma
[
51
]
.

\displaystyle\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\|\bm{B}^{R,\perp}_{t+1,h}\bm{S}^{R,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}-\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[u^{\circ}_{t,h}(Y^{L,\perp}_{t,1},\ldots,Y^{L,\perp}_{t,m},\widetilde{Y}_{0,1},\ldots,\widetilde{Y}_{0,\widetilde{m}};\mu)Y^{\top}_{0,h}]\bm{S}^{L,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}\bm{D}_{h}\|^{2}_{F}\overset{a.s.}{=}0,

(F.57)

\displaystyle\lim_{p_{h}\rightarrow\infty}\|\bm{B}^{L,\perp}_{t+1,h}\bm{S}^{L,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}-\gamma_{h}\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{h}}[v^{\circ}_{t,h}(Y^{R,\perp}_{t,h};\nu_{h})(Y^{R}_{0,h})^{\top}]\bm{S}^{R,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}\bm{D}_{h}\|^{2}_{F}\overset{a.s.}{=}0.

(F.58)

For
h\in[m]
, let us define:

\displaystyle\mathtt{S}^{L}_{t,h}(\{\bm{M}_{h}\}_{h=1}^{m},\{\bm{Q}_{h}\}_{h=1}^{m})

(F.59)

\displaystyle\equiv\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\bigg[u^{\circ}_{t,h}\bigg(\{\bm{M}_{h}U_{h}+\bm{Q}^{1/2}_{h}Z_{h}\}_{h=1}^{m},\{\bm{L}_{\ell}\widetilde{U}_{\ell}+\widetilde{Z}_{\ell}\}_{\ell=1}^{\widetilde{m}};\mu\bigg)U^{\top}_{h}\bigg]\bm{D}_{h},

(F.60)

\displaystyle\mathtt{\Sigma}^{L}_{t,h}(\{\bm{M}_{h}\}_{h=1}^{m},\{\bm{Q}_{h}\}_{h=1}^{m})\equiv\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\bigg[u^{\circ}_{t,h}\bigg(\{\bm{M}_{h}U_{h}+\bm{Q}^{1/2}_{h}Z_{h}\}_{h=1}^{m},\{\bm{L}_{\ell}\widetilde{U}_{\ell}+\widetilde{Z}_{\ell}\}_{\ell=1}^{\widetilde{m}};\mu\bigg)^{\otimes 2}\bigg],

(F.61)

\displaystyle\mathtt{S}^{R}_{t,h}(\bm{M}_{h},\bm{Q}_{h})\equiv\gamma_{h}\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{h}}[v^{\circ}_{t,h}(\bm{M}_{h}V_{h}+\bm{Q}^{1/2}_{h}Z_{h};\nu_{h})V^{\top}_{h}]\bm{D}_{h}

(F.62)

\displaystyle\mathtt{\Sigma}^{R}_{t,h}(\bm{M}_{h},\bm{Q}_{h})\equiv\gamma_{h}\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{h}}[v^{\circ}_{t,h}(\bm{M}_{h}V_{h}+\bm{Q}^{1/2}_{h}Z_{h};\nu_{h})^{\otimes 2}].

(F.63)

Let us define for
h\in[m]
, the parameters

\bm{S}^{L,\perp}_{t,h}\equiv\bm{A}^{L,\perp}_{t,h}+\bm{B}^{L,\perp}_{t,h}\bm{S}^{L,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\bm{\Sigma}^{L,\perp}_{t,h}\equiv\bm{T}^{L,\perp}_{t,h}+\bm{B}^{L,\perp}_{t,h}\bm{\Sigma}^{L,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}(\bm{B}^{L,\perp}_{t,h})^{\top}.

By construction, it is evident that for all
h\in[m]
we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\left[\psi\left(\{\bm{A}^{L,\perp}_{t,h}U_{h}+\bm{B}^{L,\perp}_{t,h}Y_{0,h}+(\bm{T}^{L,\perp}_{t,h})^{1/2}W^{\perp,L}_{t,h}\}_{h=1}^{m},\right.\right.

(F.64)

\displaystyle\hskip 100.00015pt\left.\left.\{Y_{0,h}\}_{h=1}^{m},\{\widetilde{Y}_{0,\ell}\}_{\ell=1}^{\widetilde{m}},\{U_{h}\}_{h=1}^{m},\{\widetilde{U}_{\ell}\}_{\ell=1}^{\widetilde{m}}\right)\right]

(F.65)

\displaystyle\hskip 40.00006pt=\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\left[\psi\left(\{\bm{S}^{L,\perp}_{t,h}U_{h}+(\bm{\Sigma}^{L,\perp}_{t,h})^{1/2}Z^{\perp,L}_{t,h}\}_{h=1}^{m},\right.\right.

(F.66)

\displaystyle\hskip 150.00023pt\left.\left.\{Y_{0,h}\}_{h=1}^{m},\{\widetilde{Y}_{0,\ell}\}_{\ell=1}^{\widetilde{m}},\{U_{h}\}_{h=1}^{m},\{\widetilde{U}_{\ell}\}_{\ell=1}^{\widetilde{m}}\right)\right],

(F.67)

From the definition of the functions
\mathtt{S}^{R}_{t,h}
and
\mathtt{\Sigma}^{R}_{t,h}
, using (
F.57
) we can show that

\displaystyle\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\|\bm{S}^{L,\perp}_{t,h}-\mathtt{S}^{R}_{t,h}(\bm{S}^{R,\perp}_{t,h},\bm{\Sigma}^{R,\perp}_{t,h})\|_{F}=0,

(F.68)

\displaystyle\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\|\bm{\Sigma}^{L,\perp}_{t,h}-\mathtt{\Sigma}^{R}_{t,h}(\bm{S}^{R,\perp}_{t,h},\bm{\Sigma}^{R,\perp}_{t,h})\|_{F}=0.

(F.69)

Similarly, we can show that

\displaystyle\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\|\bm{S}^{R,\perp}_{t+1,h}-\mathtt{S}^{L}_{t,h}(\{\bm{S}^{L,\perp}_{t,h}\}_{h=1}^{m},\{\bm{\Sigma}^{L,\perp}_{t,h}\}_{h=1}^{m})\|_{F}=0,

(F.70)

\displaystyle\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\|\bm{\Sigma}^{R,\perp}_{t,h}-\mathtt{\Sigma}^{L}_{t,h}(\{\bm{S}^{L,\perp}_{t,h}\}_{h=1}^{m},\{\bm{\Sigma}^{L,\perp}_{t,h}\}_{h=1}^{m})\|_{F}=0.

(F.71)

Finally, using the definition of the state evolution parameters (
5.9
), and
\bm{S}^{R,\perp}_{0,h}=\bm{S}^{R,\circ}_{0,h}
,
\bm{\Sigma}^{R,\perp}_{0,h}=\bm{\Sigma}^{R,\circ}_{0,h}
for all
h\in[m]
, using continuity of the functions
\mathtt{S}^{R}_{t,h}
and
\mathtt{\Sigma}^{R}_{t,h}
