\displaystyle\mathsf{v}(\bm{\Gamma}^{R}_{t,h};\nu_{h})=\mathbb{E}[(\mathrm{V}_{h}-\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{h}}[\mathrm{V}_{h}|\mathrm{X}^{R}(\bm{\Gamma}^{R}_{t,h})])^{\otimes 2}],

(H.2)

we have

\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{h}}[\mathrm{V}_{h}|\mathrm{X}^{R}(\bm{\Gamma}^{R}_{t,h})])^{\otimes 2}]]=\bm{I}_{r_{h}}-\mathsf{v}(\bm{\Gamma}^{R}_{t,h};\nu_{h}).

Next, recall that:

\bm{\Sigma}^{L,\circ}_{t,h}=\gamma_{h}\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{h}}[\mathrm{V}_{h}|\bm{S}^{R,\circ}_{t,h}\mathrm{V}_{h}+(\bm{\Sigma}^{R,\circ}_{t,h})^{1/2}\mathrm{Z}^{R}_{h}]^{\otimes 2}],

where
\mathrm{Z}^{R}_{h}\sim N_{r_{h}}(0,\bm{I}_{r_{h}})
and is independent of
\mathrm{V}_{h}\sim\nu_{h}
. In both the equations above and in similar contexts later, the outer expectations are always with respect to the distributions of both
\mathrm{V}_{h}
and the Gaussian random variables
\mathrm{Z}^{R}_{h}
. Again, using (
5.9
), the above equation can be re-expressed as follows:

\displaystyle\bm{\Sigma}^{L,\circ}_{t,h}

\displaystyle=\gamma_{h}\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{h}}[\mathrm{V}_{h}|\bm{D}^{1/2}_{h}\mathrm{V}_{h}+\bm{D}^{1/2}_{h}(\bm{S}^{R,\circ}_{t,h})^{-1}(\bm{\Sigma}^{R,\circ}_{t,h})^{1/2}\mathrm{Z}^{R}_{h}]^{\otimes 2}]

(H.3)

\displaystyle=\gamma_{h}\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{h}}[\mathrm{V}_{h}|\bm{D}^{1/2}_{h}\mathrm{V}_{h}+(\bm{\Gamma}^{R}_{t,h})^{-1/2}\mathrm{Z}^{R}_{h}]^{\otimes 2}].

(H.4)

Hence, we get

\displaystyle\bm{\Gamma}^{L}_{t,h}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}[\bm{D}^{1/2}_{h}\mathrm{V}_{h}|\bm{D}^{1/2}_{h}\mathrm{V}_{h}+(\bm{\Gamma}^{R}_{t,h})^{-1/2}\mathrm{Z}^{R}_{h}]^{\otimes 2}]

(H.5)

\displaystyle=\bm{D}_{h}-\mathsf{v}_{h}(\bm{\Gamma}^{R}_{t,h};\bm{D}^{1/2}_{h}\nu_{h}).

(H.6)

The other equation follows similarly.

H.2
Proof of Theorem
5.2

First, observe that
\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}[\Theta|\Theta+\bm{\Gamma}^{-1/2}Z]^{\otimes 2}]=\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}[\Theta|\bm{\Gamma}^{1/2}\Theta+Z]^{\otimes 2}]
for any
\bm{Q}
positive semi-definite and
Z\sim\mbox{Gaussian}
.
Using Lemma 9 in
[
38
]
, we can show that, for all
h\in[m]
:

\displaystyle\mathsf{v}_{h}(\bm{\Gamma}^{R}_{1};\bm{D}^{1/2}_{h}\nu_{h})\succeq\mathsf{v}_{h}(\bm{\Gamma}^{R}_{2};\bm{D}^{1/2}_{h}\nu_{h})\quad\mbox{for $\bm{\Gamma}^{R}_{1}\preceq\bm{\Gamma}^{R}_{2}$ and $\bm{\Gamma}^{R}_{1},\bm{\Gamma}^{R}_{2}\in\mathbb{S}^{r_{h}}_{+}$.}

(H.7)

Further, using the techniques used to prove Lemma 9 in
[
38
]
, we can show that for all
k\in[m]
and pairs of positive semi-definite matrices
\bm{\Gamma}^{L}_{k},\widebar{\bm{\Gamma}}^{L}_{k}\in\mathbb{S}^{r_{k}}_{+}
satisfying
\bm{\Gamma}^{L}_{k}\preceq\widebar{\bm{\Gamma}}^{L}_{k}
, if the matrices
\{\bm{\Gamma}^{L}_{g}\in\mathbb{S}^{r_{g}}_{+}:g\in[m]\setminus\{k\}\}
are fixed, then we have:

\displaystyle\mathsf{u}_{h}(\gamma_{1}\bm{\Gamma}^{L}_{1},\ldots,\gamma_{k-1}\bm{\Gamma}^{L}_{k-1},\gamma_{k}\bm{\Gamma}^{L}_{k},\gamma_{k+1}\bm{\Gamma}^{L}_{k+1},\ldots,\gamma_{m}\bm{\Gamma}^{L}_{m};\bm{D}^{1/2}\mu)

(H.8)

\displaystyle\succeq\mathsf{u}_{h}(\gamma_{1}\bm{\Gamma}^{L}_{1},\ldots,\gamma_{k-1}\bm{\Gamma}^{L}_{k-1},\gamma_{k}\widebar{\bm{\Gamma}}^{L}_{k},\gamma_{k+1}\bm{\Gamma}^{L}_{k+1},\ldots,\gamma_{m}\bm{\Gamma}^{L}_{m};\bm{D}^{1/2}\mu),

(H.9)

for all
h\in[m]
. This implies that for the collections of matrices given by
\{(\bm{\Gamma}^{L}_{h},\widebar{\bm{\Gamma}}^{L}_{h})\in\mathbb{S}^{r_{h}}_{+}\times\mathbb{S}^{r_{h}}_{+}:\bm{\Gamma}^{L}_{h}\preceq\widebar{\bm{\Gamma}}^{L}_{h},h\in[m]\}
, we have:

\displaystyle\mathsf{u}_{h}(\gamma_{1}\bm{\Gamma}^{L}_{1},\ldots,\gamma_{m}\bm{\Gamma}^{L}_{m};\bm{D}^{1/2}\mu)\succeq\mathsf{u}_{h}(\gamma_{1}\widebar{\bm{\Gamma}}^{L}_{1},\ldots,\gamma_{m}\widebar{\bm{\Gamma}}^{L}_{m};\bm{D}^{1/2}\mu),

(H.10)

for all
h\in[m]
. Further, by Lemma 9 in
[
38
]
, we get that for any
\{\bm{\Gamma}^{R}_{h}\in\mathbb{S}^{r_{h}}_{+}:h\in[m]\}
and
\{\bm{\Gamma}^{L}_{h}\in\mathbb{S}^{r_{h}}_{+}:h\in[m]\}
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{v}_{h}(\bm{\Gamma}^{R}_{h};\bm{D}^{1/2}_{h}\nu_{h})\preceq\bm{D}_{h}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\mathsf{u}_{h}(\gamma_{1}\bm{\Gamma}^{L}_{1},\ldots,\gamma_{m}\bm{\Gamma}^{L}_{m};\bm{D}^{1/2}\mu)\preceq\bm{D}_{h}.

(H.11)

Using Theorem 2 in
[
44
]
, we can show that for all
\bm{\Gamma}^{R}_{h}\in\mathbb{S}^{r_{h}}_{+}
and
h\in[m]
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{v}_{h}(\bm{\Gamma}^{R}_{h};\bm{D}^{1/2}_{h}\nu_{h})\preceq(\bm{D}^{-1}_{h}+\bm{\Gamma}^{R}_{h})^{-1}.

(H.12)

Next, for all
h\in[m]
and
\{\bm{\Gamma}^{L}_{h}\in\mathbb{S}^{r_{h}}_{+}:h\in[m]\}
, using techniques similar to the proof of Theorem 2 in
[
44
]
, we can show the following:

\displaystyle\mathsf{u}_{h}(\gamma_{1}\bm{\Gamma}^{L}_{1},\ldots,\gamma_{m}\bm{\Gamma}^{L}_{m};\bm{D}^{1/2}\mu)

(H.13)

\displaystyle\preceq(\mathsf{u}_{h}(\gamma_{1}\bm{\Gamma}^{L}_{1},\ldots,\gamma_{h-1}\bm{\Gamma}^{L}_{h-1},\bm{0},\gamma_{h+1}\bm{\Gamma}^{L}_{h+1},\ldots,\gamma_{m}\bm{\Gamma}^{L}_{m};\bm{D}^{1/2}\mu)^{-1}+\gamma_{h}\bm{\Gamma}^{L}_{h})^{-1}

(H.14)

\displaystyle\preceq(\bm{D}^{-1}_{h}+\gamma_{h}\bm{\Gamma}^{L}_{h})^{-1},

(H.15)

where the last relation follows from (
H.11
).

Next, let us recall that
\bm{\Sigma}^{R,\circ}_{0,h}=\bm{\Sigma}^{R,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}
where
\bm{\Sigma}^{R,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h}
is defined in (
2.7
) and construct
\bm{\Gamma}^{R}_{0,h}
following the prescription of (
5.24
). From (
5.25
) and Proposition 5.6 (a) of
[
59
]
, we get for all
h\in[m]

\displaystyle\bm{\Gamma}^{L}_{1,h}

\displaystyle=\bm{D}_{h}-\mathsf{v}_{h}(\bm{\Gamma}^{R}_{0,h};\bm{D}^{1/2}_{h}\nu_{h})

(H.16)

\displaystyle\succeq\bm{D}_{h}-(\bm{D}^{-1}_{h}+\bm{\Gamma}^{R}_{0,h})^{-1}=\mathsf{diag}\left(\frac{\gamma_{h}(\bm{D}^{4}_{h})_{ii}-1}{\gamma_{h}(\bm{D}_{h})_{ii}(1+(\bm{D}^{2}_{h})_{ii}}\right).

(H.17)

Therefore, again using (
5.25
) and (
H.13
), we can conclude that

\displaystyle\bm{\Gamma}^{R}_{1,h}

\displaystyle=\bm{D}_{h}-\mathsf{u}_{h}(\gamma_{1}\bm{\Gamma}^{L}_{1,1},\ldots,\gamma_{m}\bm{\Gamma}^{L}_{1,m};\bm{D}^{1/2}\mu)

(H.18)

\displaystyle\succeq\bm{D}_{h}-\left(\bm{D}^{-1}_{h}+\bm{\Gamma}^{L}_{1,h}\right)^{-1}

(H.19)

\displaystyle\succeq\bm{D}_{h}-\left(\bm{D}^{-1}_{h}+\mathsf{diag}\left(\frac{\gamma_{h}(\bm{D}^{4}_{h})_{ii}-1}{(\bm{D}_{h})_{ii}(1+(\bm{D}^{2}_{h})_{ii})}\right)\right)^{-1}=\bm{\Gamma}^{R}_{0,h},

(H.20)

for all
h\in[m]
. Again, using the definitions of
\bm{\Gamma}^{L}_{1,h}
and
\bm{\Gamma}^{L}_{0,h}
, we can conclude using (
H.7
) that
\bm{\Gamma}^{L}_{1,h}\succeq\bm{\Gamma}^{L}_{0,h}
, for all
h\in[m]
. Consequently, using (
H.7
) and (
H.10
), we can inductively conclude that for all
t\in\mathbb{N}
and
h\in[m]
,
\bm{\Gamma}^{R}_{t+1,h}\succeq\bm{\Gamma}^{R}_{t,h}
and
\bm{\Gamma}^{L}_{t+1,h}\succeq\bm{\Gamma}^{L}_{t,h}
. Further, using (
H.7
) and (
H.10
), it can also be concluded that for all
t\in\mathbb{N}
and
h\in[m]
,
\bm{\Gamma}^{R}_{t,h}\preceq\bm{D}_{h}
and
\bm{\Gamma}^{L}_{t,h}\preceq\bm{D}_{h}
. Therefore, for all
h\in[m]
, there exists matrices
\bm{\Gamma}^{R}_{\infty,h}
and
\bm{\Gamma}^{L}_{\infty,h}
, satisfying (
5.26
), such that the sequences
\{\bm{\Gamma}^{R}_{t,h}\}
and
\{\bm{\Gamma}^{L}_{t,h}\}
converges to
\bm{\Gamma}^{R}_{\infty,h}
and
\bm{\Gamma}^{L}_{\infty,h}
, respectively, as
t\rightarrow\infty
.

H.3
Proof of Theorem
5.3

By simplification, we get:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{Np_{h}}\|\bm{U}_{h}\bm{D}_{h}\bm{V}^{\top}_{h}-\widebar{\bm{U}}_{t,h}\bm{D}_{h}(\widebar{\bm{V}}_{t,h})^{\top}\|^{2}_{F}

(H.21)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{Np_{h}}\mathrm{Tr}(\bm{V}^{\top}_{h}\bm{V}_{h}\bm{D}_{h}\bm{U}^{\top}_{h}\bm{U}_{h}\bm{D}_{h})+\frac{1}{Np_{h}}\mathrm{Tr}(({\bm{V}}_{h})^{\top}\widebar{\bm{V}}_{t,h}\bm{D}_{h}(\widebar{\bm{U}}_{t,h})^{\top}\widebar{\bm{U}}_{t,h}\bm{D}_{h})

(H.22)

\displaystyle\hskip 40.00006pt-\frac{2}{Np_{h}}\mathrm{Tr}(\bm{V}^{\top}_{h}\widebar{\bm{V}}_{t,h}\bm{D}_{h}(\widebar{\bm{U}}_{t,h})^{\top}\bm{U}_{h}\bm{D}_{h}).

(H.23)

Using the Strong Law of Large Numbers, we have:

\frac{\bm{U}^{\top}_{h}\bm{U}_{h}}{N}\xrightarrow{a.s.}\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[U_{h}U^{\top}_{h}]\quad\mbox{and}\quad\frac{\bm{V}^{\top}_{h}\bm{V}_{h}}{p_{h}}\xrightarrow{a.s.}\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{h}}[V_{h}V^{\top}_{h}],\quad\mbox{for all $h\in[m]$.}

Consequently, we have:

\frac{1}{Np_{h}}\mathrm{Tr}(\bm{V}^{\top}_{h}\bm{V}_{h}\bm{D}_{h}\bm{U}^{\top}_{h}\bm{U}_{h}\bm{D}_{h})\xrightarrow{a.s.}\mathrm{Tr}\left[\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[U_{h}U^{\top}_{h}]\bm{D}_{h}\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{h}}[V_{h}V^{\top}_{h}]\bm{D}_{h}\right].

Using the assumption that
\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[U_{h}U^{\top}_{h}]=\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{h}}[V_{h}V^{\top}_{h}]=\bm{I}_{r_{h}}
for all
h\in[m]
, we have:

\frac{1}{Np_{h}}\mathrm{Tr}(\bm{V}^{\top}_{h}\bm{V}_{h}\bm{D}_{h}\bm{U}^{\top}_{h}\bm{U}_{h}\bm{D}_{h})\xrightarrow{a.s.}\mathrm{Tr}(\bm{D}^{2}_{h}).

Next, using Theorem
5.1
and (
5.9
), and the Lipschitz property of the denoisers, we get

\displaystyle\frac{(\widebar{\bm{U}}_{t,h})^{\top}\widebar{\bm{U}}_{t,h}}{N}\xrightarrow{a.s.}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\,[\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[U_{h}|Y^{\circ}_{t,1},\ldots,Y^{\circ}_{t,m},\widetilde{Y}_{0,1},\ldots,\widetilde{Y}_{0,\widetilde{m}}]^{\otimes 2}]=\bm{\Sigma}^{R,\circ}_{t+1,h},

(H.24)

\displaystyle\frac{(\widebar{\bm{V}}_{t,h})^{\top}\widebar{\bm{V}}_{t,h}}{p_{h}}\xrightarrow{a.s.}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\,[\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{h}}[V_{h}|Y^{R,\circ}_{t,h}]^{\otimes 2}]=\frac{\bm{\Sigma}^{L,\circ}_{t,h}}{\gamma_{h}}.

(H.25)

Finally, using Theorem
5.1
and
\mathbb{E}[Yg(X)|X]=g(X)\mathbb{E}[Y|X]
, we get

\displaystyle\frac{(\widebar{\bm{U}}_{t,h})^{\top}\bm{U}_{h}}{N}\xrightarrow{a.s.}\bm{\Sigma}^{R,\circ}_{t+1,h},\quad\mbox{and}\quad\frac{(\widebar{\bm{V}}_{t,h})^{\top}\bm{V}_{t,h}}{p_{h}}\xrightarrow{a.s.}\frac{\bm{\Sigma}^{L,\circ}_{t,h}}{\gamma_{h}}.

(H.26)

Plugging in (
5.9
) in (
5.24
), we get:

\bm{\Gamma}^{R}_{t,h}:=\bm{D}^{1/2}_{h}\bm{\Sigma}^{R,\circ}_{t,h}\bm{D}^{1/2}_{h},\quad\mbox{and}\quad\bm{\Gamma}^{L}_{t,h}:=\frac{1}{\gamma_{h}}\bm{D}^{1/2}_{h}\bm{\Sigma}^{L,\circ}_{t,h}\bm{D}^{1/2}_{h}.

Next, using Lemma
5.3
, Theorem
5.2
, and the fact that there exists unique fixed points to (
5.26
), we get positive definite matrices
\bm{\Gamma}^{L}_{\infty,h}
and
\bm{\Gamma}^{R}_{\infty,h}
such that:

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{t\rightarrow\infty}\bm{\Sigma}^{R,\circ}_{t,h}=\bm{D}^{-1/2}_{h}\bm{\Gamma}^{R}_{\infty,h}\bm{D}^{-1/2}_{h},\quad\mbox{and}\quad\lim\limits_{t\rightarrow\infty}\bm{\Sigma}^{L,\circ}_{t+1,h}=\gamma_{h}\bm{D}^{-1/2}_{h}\bm{\Gamma}^{L}_{\infty,h}\bm{D}^{-1/2}_{h}.

(H.27)

Substituting the above relation in (
H.24
) and then in (
H.21
), we get the desired result.

Appendix I
Proof of theoretical results for prediction sets

I.1
Proof of Theorem
5.4

Observe that as
\widehat{\bm{R}}_{h_{k}}=\frac{1}{N}(\widebar{\bm{V}}_{T,h_{k}})_{\mathcal{F}_{h_{k}}*}\widehat{\bm{D}}_{h_{k}}
,
Theorem
5.1
implies that for all
j,\ell\in[r_{h_{k}}]\times[r_{h_{k}}]
and pseudo-Lipschitz functions
\varphi_{h_{k},j,\ell}(x,v):\mathbb{R}^{2r_{h_{k}}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
defined as
\varphi_{h_{k},j,\ell}(x,v)=x_{j}x_{\ell}
,

\displaystyle\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}N\left(\left((\widebar{\bm{V}}_{T,h_{k}})_{\mathcal{F}_{h_{k}}*}\right)^{\top}_{*j}\left((\widebar{\bm{V}}_{T,h_{k}})_{\mathcal{F}_{h_{k}}*}\right)_{*\ell}\right)

\displaystyle=\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{p_{h_{k}}}{N}\times\frac{|\mathcal{F}_{h_{k}}|}{p_{h_{k}}}\times\frac{1}{|\mathcal{F}_{h_{k}}|}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{F}_{h_{k}}}\varphi_{h_{k},j,\ell}((\widebar{\bm{V}}_{T,h_{k}})_{i*},(\bm{V}_{h_{k}})_{i*})

\displaystyle\overset{a.s}{=}\,\lambda_{h_{k}}\gamma_{h_{k}}\mathbb{E}\left[\big[\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{h_{k}}}[V_{h_{k}}|Y^{R,\circ}_{T,h_{k}}]^{\otimes 2}\big]_{j\ell}\right],

where
Y^{R,\circ}_{T,h_{k}}
is defined in (
5.11
) and the outer expectation is with respect to the distribution of
Y^{R,\circ}_{T,h_{k}}
. Combining with,
\widehat{\bm{D}}_{h_{k}}\overset{a.s.}{\rightarrow}\bm{D}_{h_{k}}
, we get

\displaystyle\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}N(\widehat{\bm{R}}^{\top}_{h_{k}}\widehat{\bm{R}}_{h_{k}})\,\overset{a.s}{=}\,\lambda_{h_{k}}\gamma_{h_{k}}\bm{D}_{h_{k}}\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{h_{k}}}[V_{h_{k}}|Y^{R,\circ}_{T,h_{k}}]^{\otimes 2}\big]\bm{D}_{h_{k}}.

(I.1)

Plugging in the definition of
\bm{\Sigma}^{L,\circ}_{T,h_{k}}
from (
5.9
) in (
I.1
), we get

\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}N(\widehat{\bm{R}}^{\top}_{h_{k}}\widehat{\bm{R}}_{h_{k}})\,\overset{a.s}{=}\,\lambda_{h_{k}}\bm{D}_{h_{k}}\bm{\Sigma}^{L,\circ}_{T,h_{k}}\bm{D}_{h_{k}}.

(I.2)

Next, for any
k\in[d]
, recall that

\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}Q_{h_{k}}=\frac{1}{N}(\bm{V}_{h_{k}})_{\mathcal{F}_{h_{k}}*}\bm{D}_{h_{k}}U^{\mathrm{Q}}_{h_{k}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}W^{\mathrm{Q}}_{h_{k}}\,\in\,\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{F}_{h_{k}}|}.

Hence, for any
k\in[d]
, we have

\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}(\widehat{\bm{R}}^{\top}_{h_{k}}\widehat{\bm{R}}_{h_{k}})^{-1}\widehat{\bm{R}}^{\top}_{h_{k}}Q_{h_{k}}\,=\,\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{Q},\mathsf{signal}}_{h_{k}}+\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{Q},\mathsf{noise}}_{h_{k}},

where, conditional on
\{U^{\mathrm{Q}}_{h_{1}},\ldots,U^{\mathrm{Q}}_{h_{d}},\bar{\bm{V}}_{T,1},\ldots,\bar{\bm{V}}_{T,m},\widehat{\bm{D}}_{1},\ldots,\widehat{\bm{D}}_{m},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bm{V}_{1},\ldots,\bm{V}_{m}}\}
,

\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{Q},\mathsf{signal}}_{h_{k}}
and
\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{Q},\mathsf{noise}}_{h_{k}}
are independent and can be expressed as

\displaystyle\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{Q},\mathsf{signal}}_{h_{k}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}(\widehat{\bm{R}}^{\top}_{h_{k}}\widehat{\bm{R}}_{h_{k}})^{-1}\widehat{\bm{R}}^{\top}_{h_{k}}(\bm{V}_{h_{k}})^{\top}_{\mathcal{F}_{h_{k}*}}\bm{D}_{h_{k}}U^{\mathrm{Q}}_{h_{k}},

\displaystyle\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{Q},\mathsf{noise}}_{h_{k}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}(\widehat{\bm{R}}^{\top}_{h_{k}}\widehat{\bm{R}}_{h_{k}})^{-1}\widehat{\bm{R}}^{\top}_{h_{k}}W^{\mathrm{Q}}_{h_{k}}.

As
W^{\mathrm{Q}}_{h_{k}}\overset{ind}{\sim}N_{|\mathcal{F}_{h_{k}}|}(0,\bm{I}_{|\mathcal{F}_{h_{k}}|})
for all
k\in[d]
, we have that, conditional on

\{U^{\mathrm{Q}}_{h_{1}},\ldots,U^{\mathrm{Q}}_{h_{d}},\bm{V}_{1},\ldots,\bm{V}_{m},\bar{\bm{V}}_{T,1},\ldots,\newline
\bar{\bm{V}}_{T,m},\widehat{\bm{D}}_{1},\ldots,\widehat{\bm{D}}_{m}\},

we have

\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{Q},\mathsf{noise}}_{h_{k}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle ind}}{{\sim}}N_{r_{h_{k}}}(0,\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{Q},H}_{h_{k}}),\quad k\in[d],

where for each
k
,

\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{Q},H}_{h_{k}}=\frac{(\widehat{\bm{R}}^{\top}_{h_{k}}\widehat{\bm{R}}_{h_{k}})^{-1}}{N}\widehat{\bm{D}}_{h_{k}}\frac{(\widebar{\bm{V}}_{T,h_{k}})^{\top}_{\mathcal{F}_{h_{k}*}}(\widebar{\bm{V}}_{T,h_{k}})_{\mathcal{F}_{h_{k}*}}}{N}\widehat{\bm{D}}_{h_{k}}\frac{(\widehat{\bm{R}}^{\top}_{h_{k}}\widehat{\bm{R}}_{h_{k}})^{-1}}{N}.

In view of (
I.2
),

\lim_{N\to\infty}\bm{\Sigma}^{\mathrm{Q},H}_{h_{k}}=\lambda_{h_{k}}^{-1}\bm{D}_{h_{k}}^{-1}(\bm{\Sigma}^{L,\circ}_{T,h_{k}})^{-1}\bm{D}_{h_{k}}^{-1}.

(I.3)

Using Theorem
5.1
again,
we obtain that

\displaystyle\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{Q},\mathsf{signal}}_{h_{k}}\,\overset{a.s.}{=}\,U^{\mathrm{Q}}_{h_{k}}.

(I.4)

Combining
(
I.3
)

and (
I.4
), we get the stated assertion.

I.2
Proof of Lemma
5.4

Since
\widehat{\mu}=\widehat{\mu}_{N}\xrightarrow{w}\mu
, using arguments similar to Lemma
E.1
, we obtain that
the sequence of probability measures
\{\widehat{\mu}:N\in\mathbb{N}\}
is uniformly tight.
Hence, for all
\varepsilon>0
, there exists a constant
M_{\mu,\varepsilon}>0
, such that for all
N\in\mathbb{N}
,

\widehat{\mu}(B_{r+\widetilde{r}}(0,M_{\mu,\varepsilon}))\geq 1-\frac{\varepsilon}{8}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\mu(B_{r+\widetilde{r}}(0,M_{\mu,\varepsilon}))\geq 1-\frac{\varepsilon}{8}.

This implies that, for all
N\in\mathbb{N}
and
C\in\mathscr{C}_{r+\widetilde{r}}
,

\displaystyle\widehat{\mu}(C)-\mu(C)

\displaystyle=\widehat{\mu}(B_{r+\widetilde{r}}(0,M_{\mu,\varepsilon})\cap C)-\mu(B_{r+\widetilde{r}}(0,M_{\mu,\varepsilon})\cap C)

(I.5)

\displaystyle+\widehat{\mu}(B^{c}_{r+\widetilde{r}}(0,M_{\mu,\varepsilon})\cap C)-\mu(B^{c}_{r+\widetilde{r}}(0,M_{\mu,\varepsilon})\cap C)

\displaystyle\leq\widehat{\mu}(B_{r+\widetilde{r}}(0,M_{\mu,\varepsilon})\cap C)-\mu(B_{r+\widetilde{r}}(0,M_{\mu,\varepsilon})\cap C)+\frac{\varepsilon}{4}.

Thus, it suffices to work with the following collection of sets

\mathscr{C}_{r+\widetilde{r},M_{\mu,\varepsilon}}=\{C\cap B_{r+\widetilde{r}}(0,M_{\mu,\varepsilon}):C\in\mathscr{C}_{r+\widetilde{r}}\}.

Note that this is a collection of compact and convex subsets of a bounded set in
\mathbb{R}^{r+\widetilde{r}}
.
Since
\mu
is absolutely continuous with respect to the Lebesgue measure with bounded density, Corollary 2.7.9 of
[
55
]
implies that the collection
\mathscr{C}_{r+\widetilde{r},M_{\mu,\varepsilon}}
has a finite bracketing number with respect to
\mu
.
In other words, for any
\varepsilon^{\prime}>0
, there exists a natural number,
N_{[\,]}(\mathscr{C}_{r+\widetilde{r},M_{\mu,\varepsilon}},\varepsilon^{\prime},\mu)<\infty
and a collection of pairs of sets,
\mathscr{C}_{r+\widetilde{r},M_{\mu,\varepsilon},\varepsilon^{\prime},[\,]}=\left\{(C_{L,1},C_{U,1}),\ldots,(C_{L,N_{[\,]}(\mathscr{C}_{r+\widetilde{r},M_{\mu,\varepsilon}},\varepsilon^{\prime},\mu)},C_{U,N_{[\,]}(\mathscr{C}_{r+\widetilde{r},M_{\mu,\varepsilon}},\varepsilon^{\prime},\mu)})\right\}\subseteq\mathscr{C}_{r+\widetilde{r},M_{\mu,\varepsilon}}\times\mathscr{C}_{r+\widetilde{r},M_{\mu,\varepsilon}}
, such that for any
C\in\mathscr{C}_{r+\widetilde{r},M_{\mu,\varepsilon}}
, we have pairs of sets
(C_{L,k},C_{U,k})\in\mathscr{C}_{r+\widetilde{r},M_{\mu,\varepsilon},\varepsilon^{\prime},[\,]}
satisfying

C_{L,k}\subseteq C\subseteq C_{U,k},\quad\mbox{and}\quad\mu(C_{U,k}\setminus C_{L,k})<\frac{\varepsilon^{\prime}}{4}.

Consequently, for a set in
C\in\mathscr{C}_{r+\widetilde{r},M_{\mu,\varepsilon}}
, if its
\varepsilon^{\prime}
-bracket is given by
(C_{L,k},C_{U,k})
, then we have

\displaystyle\widehat{\mu}(C)-\mu(C)

\displaystyle\leq\widehat{\mu}(C_{U,k})-\mu(C_{U,k})+\mu(C_{U,k})-\mu(C)

(I.6)

\displaystyle\leq\widehat{\mu}(C_{U,k})-\mu(C_{U,k})+\frac{\varepsilon^{\prime}}{4}

(I.7)

\displaystyle\leq\sup\left\{\widehat{\mu}(D_{U})-\mu(D_{U}):(D_{L},D_{U})\in\mathscr{C}_{r+\widetilde{r},M_{\mu,\varepsilon},\varepsilon^{\prime},[\,]}\right\}+\frac{\varepsilon^{\prime}}{4}.

(I.8)

This implies,

\sup_{C\in\mathscr{C}_{r+\widetilde{r},M_{\mu,\varepsilon}}}\left\{\widehat{\mu}(C)-\mu(C)\right\}\leq\sup\left\{\widehat{\mu}(D_{U})-\mu(D_{U}):(D_{L},D_{U})\in\mathscr{C}_{r+\widetilde{r},M_{\mu,\varepsilon},\varepsilon^{\prime},[\,]}\right\}+\frac{\varepsilon^{\prime}}{4}.

Similarly, we can show that

\inf_{C\in\mathscr{C}_{r+\widetilde{r},M_{\mu,\varepsilon}}}\left\{\widehat{\mu}(C)-\mu(C)\right\}\geq\inf\left\{\widehat{\mu}(D_{L})-\mu(D_{L}):(D_{L},D_{U})\in\mathscr{C}_{r+\widetilde{r},M_{\mu,\varepsilon},\varepsilon^{\prime},[\,]}\right\}-\frac{\varepsilon^{\prime}}{4}.

Combining the last two displays and setting
\varepsilon^{\prime}=\varepsilon
,
we obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{C\in\mathscr{C}_{r+\widetilde{r},M_{\mu,\varepsilon}}}\left|\widehat{\mu}(C)-\mu(C)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\max\bigg\{\sup_{(D_{L},D_{U})\in\mathscr{C}_{r+\widetilde{r},M_{\mu,\varepsilon},\varepsilon,[\,]}}\left\{\widehat{\mu}(D_{U})-\mu(D_{U})\right\},

(I.9)

\displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt-\inf_{(D_{L},D_{U})\in\mathscr{C}_{r+\widetilde{r},M_{\mu,\varepsilon},\varepsilon,[\,]}}\left\{\widehat{\mu}(D_{L})-\mu(D_{L})\right\}\bigg\}+\frac{\varepsilon}{2}.

(I.10)

Since,
\mu
is absolutely continuous with respect to Lebesgue measure and supported on
\mathbb{R}^{r+\widetilde{r}}
, it assigns zero mass to the boundaries of compact sets. Consequently, as
|\mathscr{C}_{r+\widetilde{r},M_{\mu,\varepsilon},\varepsilon,[\,]}|<\infty
and
\widehat{\mu}\xrightarrow{w}\mu
,
we have

\limsup_{N\rightarrow\infty}\sup_{C\in\mathscr{C}_{r+\widetilde{r},M_{\mu,\varepsilon}}}\left|\widehat{\mu}(C)-\mu(C)\right|\leq\frac{3\varepsilon}{4},\quad\mbox{almost surely.}

Substituting the above inequality in (
I.5
), we almost surely get

\limsup_{N\rightarrow\infty}\sup_{C\in\mathscr{C}_{r+\widetilde{r}}}\left|\widehat{\mu}(C)-\mu(C)\right|\leq\varepsilon.

Since,
\varepsilon>0
is arbitrary, the conclusion of the lemma follows.

I.3
Proof of Theorem
5.5

Let us observe that by definition, for
y^{\mathrm{Q}}\in\mathbb{R}^{r^{\mathrm{Q}}}
and

\widetilde{y}^{\mathrm{Q}}\in\mathbb{R}^{\widetilde{r}^{\mathrm{Q}}}
satisfying (
2.32
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\widehat{\mu}}\left[(U^{\mathrm{Q}}_{1},\ldots,U^{\mathrm{Q}}_{m},\widetilde{U}^{\mathrm{Q}}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{U}^{\mathrm{Q}}_{\widetilde{m}})\in\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}\,\Big|\,Y^{\mathrm{Q}}=y^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}}=\widetilde{y}^{\mathrm{Q}}\right]\geq 1-\alpha,

(I.11)

where
Y^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}}
are defined in (
2.30
) with prior
\widehat{\mu}
.
Let us define the function:

f(x;B,\pi):=\mathbb{P}_{\pi}\left[(U^{\mathrm{Q}}_{1},\ldots,U^{\mathrm{Q}}_{m},\widetilde{U}^{\mathrm{Q}}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{U}^{\mathrm{Q}}_{\widetilde{m}})\in B\,\Big|\,(Y^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}})=x\right].

In the above definition,
x\in\mathbb{R}^{r^{\mathrm{Q}}+\widetilde{r}^{\mathrm{Q}}}
,
B\subset\mathbb{R}^{r+\widetilde{r}}
and
(Y^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}})
satisfy (
2.30
) with

(U^{\mathrm{Q}}_{h_{1}},\ldots,U^{\mathrm{Q}}_{h_{d}},\widetilde{U}^{\mathrm{Q}}_{\ell_{1}},\ldots,\widetilde{U}^{\mathrm{Q}}_{\ell_{\widetilde{d}}})\sim\pi_{h_{1},\ldots,h_{d};\ell_{1},\ldots,\ell_{\widetilde{d}}}.

Here,
\pi_{h_{1},\ldots,h_{d};\ell_{1},\ldots,\ell_{\widetilde{d}}}
is the marginal distribution of the modalities
h_{1},\ldots,h_{d}
and
\ell_{1},\ldots,\ell_{\widetilde{d}}
when the entire set of latent factors
(U^{\mathrm{Q}}_{1},\ldots,U^{\mathrm{Q}}_{m},\widetilde{U}^{\mathrm{Q}}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{U}^{\mathrm{Q}}_{\widetilde{m}})\sim\pi
.
Now, Theorem
5.4
, and Skorokhod’s representation theorem implies that

y^{\mathrm{Q}}=s^{\mathrm{Q}}+e^{\mathrm{Q}}
, where
s^{\mathrm{Q}}
is a sample from the distribution of
Y^{\mathrm{Q}}
and
e^{\mathrm{Q}}
is a realization of a random variable
E^{\mathrm{Q}}=(E^{\mathrm{Q}}_{h_{1}},\ldots,E^{\mathrm{Q}}_{h_{d}})\in\mathbb{R}^{r^{\mathrm{Q}}}
defined as follows:

E^{\mathrm{Q}}_{h_{k}}=\left(\frac{1}{N}(\widehat{\bm{R}}^{\top}_{h_{k}}\widehat{\bm{R}}_{h_{k}})^{-1}\widehat{\bm{R}}^{\top}_{h_{k}}(\bm{V}_{h_{k}})^{\top}_{\mathcal{F}_{h_{k}*}}\bm{D}_{h_{k}}-\bm{I}_{r_{h_{k}}}\right)U^{\mathrm{Q}}_{h_{k}}+\bm{T}_{h_{k}}Z_{h_{k}}.

In the above definition, the random matrix
\bm{T}_{h_{k}}\xrightarrow{a.s}0
and
Z_{h_{k}}\sim N_{r_{h_{k}}}(0,\bm{I}_{r_{h_{k}}})
.
Conditional on the matrices
\{\bar{\bm{V}}_{T,1},\ldots,\bar{\bm{V}}_{T,m},\widehat{\bm{D}}_{1},\ldots,\widehat{\bm{D}}_{m},\bm{V}_{1},\ldots,\bm{V}_{m}\}
,

\displaystyle E^{\mathrm{Q}}_{h_{k}}\sim N\left(\left(\frac{1}{N}(\widehat{\bm{R}}^{\top}_{h_{k}}\widehat{\bm{R}}_{h_{k}})^{-1}\widehat{\bm{R}}^{\top}_{h_{k}}(\bm{V}_{h_{k}})^{\top}_{\mathcal{F}_{h_{k}*}}\bm{D}_{h_{k}}-\bm{I}_{r_{h_{k}}}\right)U^{\mathrm{Q}}_{h_{k}},\bm{T}_{h_{k}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\bm{T}}_{h_{k}}^{\top}}\right),

(I.12)

and all quantities defining mean and variance in the last display, except for
U^{\mathrm{Q}}_{h_{k}}
, are deterministic after conditioning.
However, from Lemma
5.1
and Theorem
5.1
, we can conclude that

\frac{1}{N}(\widehat{\bm{R}}^{\top}_{h_{k}}\widehat{\bm{R}}_{h_{k}})^{-1}\widehat{\bm{R}}^{\top}_{h_{k}}(\bm{V}_{h_{k}})^{\top}_{\mathcal{F}_{h_{k}*}}\bm{D}_{h_{k}}-\bm{I}_{r_{h_{k}}}\xrightarrow{a.s}0.

Henceforth, we shall condition on the the reference data

\{\bar{\bm{V}}_{T,1},\ldots,\bar{\bm{V}}_{T,m},\widehat{\bm{D}}_{1},\ldots,\widehat{\bm{D}}_{m},\bm{V}_{1},\ldots,\bm{V}_{m}\}.

Since, almost surely
\widehat{\mu}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle w}}{{\to}}\mu
, for a given
\varepsilon>0
, we can get a constant
K>0
(independent of
N
) such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\widehat{\mu}}[\|(U^{\mathrm{Q}}_{h_{1}},\ldots,U^{\mathrm{Q}}_{h_{d}})\|>K]<\frac{\varepsilon}{16}\quad\mbox{a.s.}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\mathbb{P}_{\mu}[\|(U^{\mathrm{Q}}_{h_{1}},\ldots,U^{\mathrm{Q}}_{h_{d}})\|>K]<\frac{\varepsilon}{16}.

(I.13)

Therefore, by (
I.12
), (
I.13
) and the same
\varepsilon>0
chosen in (
I.13
), we can get a constant
\widebar{M}>0
(independent of
N
) such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\widehat{\mu}}[\|E^{\mathrm{Q}}\|>\widebar{M}]<\frac{\varepsilon}{16}\quad\mbox{a.s.}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\mathbb{P}_{\mu}[\|E^{\mathrm{Q}}\|>\widebar{M}]<\frac{\varepsilon}{16}.

(I.14)

Henceforth, we shall use

\mathbb{P}_{\widehat{\mu}}\left[(U^{\mathrm{Q}}_{1},\ldots,U^{\mathrm{Q}}_{m},\widetilde{U}^{\mathrm{Q}}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{U}^{\mathrm{Q}}_{\widetilde{m}})\in\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}\,\Big|\,Y^{\mathrm{Q}}+E^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}}\right]

as a (slightly abused) alternative notation for
f((Y^{\mathrm{Q}}+E^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}});\mathcal{C}_{\alpha},\widehat{\mu})
and

\mathbb{P}_{\mu}\left[(U^{\mathrm{Q}}_{1},\ldots,U^{\mathrm{Q}}_{m},\widetilde{U}^{\mathrm{Q}}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{U}^{\mathrm{Q}}_{\widetilde{m}})\in\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}\,\Big|\,Y^{\mathrm{Q}}+E^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}}\right]

for
f((Y^{\mathrm{Q}}+E^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}});\mathcal{C}_{\alpha},\mu)
.
From (
I.11
), we have

\mathbb{E}_{\widehat{\mu}}\left[\mathbb{P}_{\widehat{\mu}}\left[(U^{\mathrm{Q}}_{1},\ldots,U^{\mathrm{Q}}_{m},\widetilde{U}^{\mathrm{Q}}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{U}^{\mathrm{Q}}_{\widetilde{m}})\in\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}\,\Big|\,Y^{\mathrm{Q}}+E^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}}\right]\right]\geq 1-\alpha.

We shall show that, as
N\rightarrow\infty
,

\displaystyle\bigg|\mathbb{E}_{\widehat{\mu}}\left[\mathbb{P}_{\widehat{\mu}}\left[(U^{\mathrm{Q}}_{1},\ldots,U^{\mathrm{Q}}_{m},\widetilde{U}^{\mathrm{Q}}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{U}^{\mathrm{Q}}_{\widetilde{m}})\in\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}\,\Big|\,Y^{\mathrm{Q}}+E^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}}\right]\right]

(I.15)

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}_{\widehat{\mu}}\left[\mathbb{P}_{\widehat{\mu}}\left[(U^{\mathrm{Q}}_{1},\ldots,U^{\mathrm{Q}}_{m},\widetilde{U}^{\mathrm{Q}}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{U}^{\mathrm{Q}}_{\widetilde{m}})\in\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}\,\Big|\,Y^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}}\right]\right]\bigg|\xrightarrow{a.s}0.

This implies

\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{P}_{\widehat{\mu}}\left[(U^{\mathrm{Q}}_{1},\ldots,U^{\mathrm{Q}}_{m},\widetilde{U}^{\mathrm{Q}}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{U}^{\mathrm{Q}}_{\widetilde{m}})\in\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}\right]\geq 1-\alpha,\quad\mbox{a.s.}

Now since
\widehat{\mu}\xrightarrow{w}\mu
, the sequences of random variables
\{(Y^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}})\}
and
\{(Y^{\mathrm{Q}}+E^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}})\}
are tight. This implies, for the
\varepsilon>0
chosen in (
I.13
), there exist compact and convex sets
M\in\mathbb{R}^{r^{\mathrm{Q}}+\widetilde{r}^{\mathrm{Q}}}
and
\widetilde{M}\in\mathbb{R}^{r+\widetilde{r}}
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\widehat{\mu}}[(Y^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}})\notin M]<\frac{\varepsilon}{16},\quad\mathbb{P}_{\widehat{\mu}}[(Y^{\mathrm{Q}}+E^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}})\notin M]<\frac{\varepsilon}{16},

(I.16)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mu}[(Y^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}})\notin M]<\frac{\varepsilon}{16},\quad\mathbb{P}_{\mu}[(Y^{\mathrm{Q}}+E^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}})\notin M]<\frac{\varepsilon}{16},

\displaystyle\widehat{\mu}(\widetilde{M}^{c})<\frac{\varepsilon}{16},\quad\mbox{and}\quad\mu(\widetilde{M}^{c})<\frac{\varepsilon}{16}.

In the above definition,
\mathbb{P}_{\mu}[(Y^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}})\in\cdot]
refers to the probability of
(Y^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}})
when in the definition (
2.30
), the latent factors
(U^{\mathrm{Q}}_{1},\ldots,U^{\mathrm{Q}}_{m},\widetilde{U}^{\mathrm{Q}}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{U}^{\mathrm{Q}}_{\widetilde{m}})\sim\mu
.
By properties of multivariate Gaussian distributions,
for any compact set
M\in\mathbb{R}^{r^{\mathrm{Q}}+\widetilde{r}^{\mathrm{Q}}}
and
t\in\mathbb{R}^{r^{\mathrm{Q}}}
satisfying
\|t\|\leq\widebar{M}
(with
\widebar{M}
defined in (
I.14
)), the function

\displaystyle f((y+t,\widetilde{y});B,\pi)

\displaystyle\hskip 60.00009pt:=\mathbb{P}_{\pi}\left[(U^{\mathrm{Q}}_{1},\ldots,U^{\mathrm{Q}}_{m},\widetilde{U}^{\mathrm{Q}}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{U}^{\mathrm{Q}}_{\widetilde{m}})\in B\,\Big|\,(Y^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}})=(y+t,\widetilde{y})\right]\mathbbm{1}_{[(y,\widetilde{y})\in M]},

is uniformly Lipschitz and bounded as a function of
(y,\widetilde{y})\in M
.
By Corollary 2.7.2 of
[
55
]
, the class of functions

\mathcal{F}_{M}=\bigg\{f((y+t,{\widetilde{y}});B,\pi)\mathbbm{1}_{[(y,\widetilde{y})\in M]}:\pi\in\{\widehat{\mu},\mu\},\,B\subset\widetilde{M},\,\|t\|\leq\widebar{M}\bigg\},

has a finite bracketing number.
Therefore, proceeding as in the proof of Lemma
5.4
, we can show that

\displaystyle\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\pi\in\{\widehat{\mu},\mu\}\\
B\subset\widetilde{M},\|t\|\leq\widebar{M}\end{subarray}}\bigg|\mathbb{E}_{\widehat{\mu}}\left[f((Y^{\mathrm{Q}}+t,\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}}),B,\pi)\mathbbm{1}_{[(Y^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}})\in M]}\right]

(I.17)

\displaystyle\hskip 100.00015pt-\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\left[f((Y^{\mathrm{Q}}+t,\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}}),B,\pi)\mathbbm{1}_{[(Y^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}})\in M]}\right]\bigg|\xrightarrow{a.s}0.

(I.18)

This implies

\displaystyle\bigg|\mathbb{E}_{\widehat{\mu}}\left[f((Y^{\mathrm{Q}}+E^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}}),\mathcal{C}_{\alpha},\widehat{\mu})\mathbbm{1}_{[(Y^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}})\in M,\,\|E^{\mathrm{Q}}\|\leq\widebar{M}]}\right]

(I.19)

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\left[f((Y^{\mathrm{Q}}+E^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}}),\mathcal{C}_{\alpha},\widehat{\mu})\mathbbm{1}_{[(Y^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}})\in M,\,\|E^{\mathrm{Q}}\|\leq\widebar{M}]}\right]\bigg|\xrightarrow{a.s}0.

Similarly, we can show that

\displaystyle\bigg|\mathbb{E}_{\widehat{\mu}}\left[f((Y^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}}),\mathcal{C}_{\alpha},\widehat{\mu})\mathbbm{1}_{[(Y^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}})\in M]}\right]

(I.20)

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\left[f((Y^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}}),\mathcal{C}_{\alpha},\widehat{\mu})\mathbbm{1}_{[(Y^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}})\in M]}\right]\bigg|\xrightarrow{a.s}0.

Now, let us consider the following:

\displaystyle\bigg|\mathbb{E}_{\widehat{\mu}}\left[\mathbb{P}_{\widehat{\mu}}\left[(U^{\mathrm{Q}}_{1},\ldots,U^{\mathrm{Q}}_{m},\widetilde{U}^{\mathrm{Q}}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{U}^{\mathrm{Q}}_{\widetilde{m}})\in\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}\,\Big|\,Y^{\mathrm{Q}}+E^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}}\right]\right]

(I.21)

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}_{\widehat{\mu}}\left[\mathbb{P}_{\widehat{\mu}}\left[(U^{\mathrm{Q}}_{1},\ldots,U^{\mathrm{Q}}_{m},\widetilde{U}^{\mathrm{Q}}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{U}^{\mathrm{Q}}_{\widetilde{m}})\in\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}\,\Big|\,Y^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}}\right]\right]\bigg|

\displaystyle=\bigg|\mathbb{E}_{\widehat{\mu}}\left[\mathbb{P}_{\widehat{\mu}}\left[(U^{\mathrm{Q}}_{1},\ldots,U^{\mathrm{Q}}_{m},\widetilde{U}^{\mathrm{Q}}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{U}^{\mathrm{Q}}_{\widetilde{m}})\in\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}\,\Big|\,Y^{\mathrm{Q}}+E^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}}\right]\mathbbm{1}_{[(Y^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}})\notin M]}\right]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}_{\widehat{\mu}}\left[\mathbb{P}_{\widehat{\mu}}\left[(U^{\mathrm{Q}}_{1},\ldots,U^{\mathrm{Q}}_{m},\widetilde{U}^{\mathrm{Q}}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{U}^{\mathrm{Q}}_{\widetilde{m}})\in\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}\,\Big|\,Y^{\mathrm{Q}}+E^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}}\right]\mathbbm{1}_{[(Y^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}})\in M]}\mathbbm{1}_{[\|E^{\mathrm{Q}}\|>\widebar{M}]}\right]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}_{\widehat{\mu}}\left[\mathbb{P}_{\widehat{\mu}}\left[(U^{\mathrm{Q}}_{1},\ldots,U^{\mathrm{Q}}_{m},\widetilde{U}^{\mathrm{Q}}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{U}^{\mathrm{Q}}_{\widetilde{m}})\in\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}\,\Big|\,Y^{\mathrm{Q}}+E^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}}\right]\mathbbm{1}_{[(Y^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}})\in M,\|E^{\mathrm{Q}}\|\leq\widebar{M}]}\right]

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}_{\widehat{\mu}}\left[\mathbb{P}_{\widehat{\mu}}\left[(U^{\mathrm{Q}}_{1},\ldots,U^{\mathrm{Q}}_{m},\widetilde{U}^{\mathrm{Q}}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{U}^{\mathrm{Q}}_{\widetilde{m}})\in\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}\,\Big|\,Y^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}}\right]\mathbbm{1}_{[(Y^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}})\in M]}\right]

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}_{\widehat{\mu}}\left[\mathbb{P}_{\widehat{\mu}}\left[(U^{\mathrm{Q}}_{1},\ldots,U^{\mathrm{Q}}_{m},\widetilde{U}^{\mathrm{Q}}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{U}^{\mathrm{Q}}_{\widetilde{m}})\in\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}\,\Big|\,Y^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}}\right]\mathbbm{1}_{[(Y^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}})\notin M]}\right]\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq 2\,\mathbb{P}_{\widehat{\mu}}[(Y^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}})\notin M]+\mathbb{P}_{\widehat{\mu}}[\|E^{\mathrm{Q}}\|>\widebar{M}]+T_{1}+T_{2}+T_{3}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{4}+T_{1}+T_{2}+T_{3},

where

\displaystyle T_{1}

\displaystyle=\bigg|\mathbb{E}_{\widehat{\mu}}\left[f((Y^{\mathrm{Q}}+E^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}}),\mathcal{C}_{\alpha},\widehat{\mu})\mathbbm{1}_{[(Y^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}})\in M,\,\|E^{\mathrm{Q}}\|\leq\widebar{M}]}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 60.00009pt-\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\left[f((Y^{\mathrm{Q}}+E^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}}),\mathcal{C}_{\alpha},\widehat{\mu})\mathbbm{1}_{[(Y^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}})\in M,\,\|E^{\mathrm{Q}}\|\leq\widebar{M}]}\right]\bigg|,

\displaystyle T_{2}

\displaystyle=\bigg|\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\left[f((Y^{\mathrm{Q}}+E^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}}),\mathcal{C}_{\alpha},\widehat{\mu})\mathbbm{1}_{[(Y^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}})\in M,\,\|E^{\mathrm{Q}}\|\leq\widebar{M}]}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 100.00015pt-\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\left[f((Y^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}}),\mathcal{C}_{\alpha},\widehat{\mu})\mathbbm{1}_{[(Y^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}})\in M]}\right]\bigg|,

\displaystyle T_{3}

\displaystyle=\bigg|\mathbb{E}_{\widehat{\mu}}\left[f((Y^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}}),\mathcal{C}_{\alpha},\widehat{\mu})\mathbbm{1}_{[(Y^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}})\in M]}\right]-\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\left[f((Y^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}}),\mathcal{C}_{\alpha},\widehat{\mu})\mathbbm{1}_{[(Y^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}})\in M]}\right]\bigg|.

By (
I.19
), we can conclude that
T_{1}\xrightarrow{a.s}0
and by (
I.20
), we can conclude that
T_{3}\xrightarrow{a.s}0
. Next, by the properties of multivariate Gaussian distributions and arguments similar to those leading to (
I.19
),
we can also show that, as
N\rightarrow\infty
,

\displaystyle\bigg|f((Y^{\mathrm{Q}}+E^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}}),\mathcal{C}_{\alpha},\widehat{\mu})\mathbbm{1}_{[(Y^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}})\in M,\,\|E^{\mathrm{Q}}\|\leq\widebar{M}]}

\displaystyle\hskip 100.00015pt-f((Y^{\mathrm{Q}}+E^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}}),\mathcal{C}_{\alpha},\mu)\mathbbm{1}_{[(Y^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}})\in M,\,\|E^{\mathrm{Q}}\|\leq\widebar{M}]}\bigg|\xrightarrow{a.s}0,

\displaystyle\bigg|f((Y^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}}),\mathcal{C}_{\alpha},\widehat{\mu})\mathbbm{1}_{[(Y^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}})\in M]}-f((Y^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}}),\mathcal{C}_{\alpha},\mu)\mathbbm{1}_{[(Y^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}})\in M]}\bigg|\xrightarrow{a.s}0.

Finally, as
\|E^{\mathrm{Q}}\|\xrightarrow{a.s}0
, by the Dominated Convergence theorem and Lemma
5.1
, we conclude that

\bigg|f((Y^{\mathrm{Q}}+E^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}}),\mathcal{C}_{\alpha},\mu)\mathbbm{1}_{[(Y^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}})\in M,\,\|E^{\mathrm{Q}}\|\leq\widebar{M}]}-f((Y^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}}),\mathcal{C}_{\alpha},\mu)\mathbbm{1}_{[(Y^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}})\in M]}\bigg|\xrightarrow{a.s}0.

Therefore,

\bigg|f((Y^{\mathrm{Q}}+E^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}}),\mathcal{C}_{\alpha},\widehat{\mu})\mathbbm{1}_{[(Y^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}})\in M,\,\|E^{\mathrm{Q}}\|\leq\widebar{M}]}-f((Y^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}}),\mathcal{C}_{\alpha},\widehat{\mu})\mathbbm{1}_{[(Y^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}})\in M]}\bigg|\xrightarrow{a.s}0.

Furthermore, since the functions in the above equation are uniformly dominated by
1
, by the Dominated Convergence Theorem, we have

\displaystyle\bigg|\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\left[f((Y^{\mathrm{Q}}+E^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}}),\mathcal{C}_{\alpha},\widehat{\mu})\mathbbm{1}_{[(Y^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}})\in M,\,\|E^{\mathrm{Q}}\|\leq\widebar{M}]}\right]

(I.22)

\displaystyle\hskip 100.00015pt-\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\left[f((Y^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}}),\mathcal{C}_{\alpha},\widehat{\mu})\mathbbm{1}_{[(Y^{\mathrm{Q}},\widetilde{Y}^{\mathrm{Q}})\in M]}\right]\bigg|\rightarrow 0,

(I.23)

almost surely, given
\{\bar{\bm{V}}_{T,1},\ldots,\bar{\bm{V}}_{T,m},\widehat{\bm{D}}_{1},\ldots,\widehat{\bm{D}}_{m},\bm{V}_{1},\ldots,\bm{V}_{m}\}
. In other words,
T_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle a.s.}}{{\to}}0
conditionally.
Since,
\varepsilon
chosen in (
I.14
) is arbitrary, by (
I.21
), we can get (
I.15
) conditioning on

\{\bar{\bm{V}}_{T,1},\ldots,\bar{\bm{V}}_{T,m},\widehat{\bm{D}}_{1},\ldots,\widehat{\bm{D}}_{m},\bm{V}_{1},\ldots,\bm{V}_{m}\}.

As the conclusion holds regardless of the conditioning event, (
I.15
) holds marginally.
Now, as
\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}\in\mathscr{C}_{r+\widetilde{r}}
, Lemma
5.4

implies,

\displaystyle|\mu(\mathcal{C}_{\alpha})-\widehat{\mu}(\mathcal{C}_{\alpha})|\xrightarrow{a.s}0,\quad\mbox{as $N\rightarrow\infty$,}

(I.24)

conditioned on
\{\bar{\bm{V}}_{T,1},\ldots,\bar{\bm{V}}_{T,m},\widehat{\bm{D}}_{1},\ldots,\widehat{\bm{D}}_{m},\bm{V}_{1},\ldots,\bm{V}_{m}\}
. Since the right-hand side of (
I.24
) is non-random, (
I.24
) also holds unconditionally.
Hence,
we almost surely have

\displaystyle\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{P}_{\mu}\left[(U^{\mathrm{Q}}_{1},\ldots,U^{\mathrm{Q}}_{m},\widetilde{U}^{\mathrm{Q}}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{U}^{\mathrm{Q}}_{\widetilde{m}})\in\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}\right]

\displaystyle=\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\mu(\mathcal{C}_{\alpha})\geq\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\widehat{\mu}(\mathcal{C}_{\alpha})+\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}|}\widehat{\mu}(\mathcal{C}_{\alpha})-\mu(\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}|}\geq 1-\alpha.

Again as,
\mathbb{P}_{\mu}\left[(U^{\mathrm{Q}}_{1},\ldots,U^{\mathrm{Q}}_{m},\widetilde{U}^{\mathrm{Q}}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{U}^{\mathrm{Q}}_{\widetilde{m}})\in\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}\right]
is a non-random quantity, our result follows.

Appendix J
Choice of prior classes

In this section, we provide examples of two classes of prior distributions that can be chosen as
\mathcal{P}
and
\mathcal{P}_{\nu_{h}}
.

J.1
Gaussian mixture models

We can choose
\mathcal{P}
in (
2.13
) and each
\mathcal{P}_{\nu_{h}}
in (
2.15
) to be the set of distributions on
\mathbb{R}^{r+\widetilde{r}}
and
\mathbb{R}^{r_{h}}
respectively, which are absolutely continuous with respect to the Lebesgue measure and have bounded densities.
From
[
21
, Chapter 3]
, we know that the class of Gaussian mixture distributions with a large number of components can approximate any distribution up to negligible error.
Inspired by this fact,
we can set
\mathcal{P}
and all
\mathcal{P}_{\nu_{h}}
’s as collections of Gaussian mixture distributions with large numbers of components.
The maximum number of components in each class can be specified according to the size of the data and its complexity.
In other words,
we can set
\mathcal{P}
as

{\mathcal{P}}=\left\{\sum_{k=1}^{K}\pi_{k}\,N_{r+\widetilde{r}}(m_{k},\bm{\Sigma}_{k}):m_{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{r+\widetilde{r}},\,\bm{\Sigma}_{k}\in\mathbb{S}^{r+\widetilde{r}}_{+},\,\pi_{k}\geq 0,\,\sum_{k=1}^{K}\pi_{k}=1\;\mbox{for $k\in[K]$}\right\},

(J.1)

and
\mathcal{P}_{\nu_{h}}
for each
h\in[m]
as

\displaystyle{\mathcal{P}}_{\nu_{h}}

\displaystyle=\left\{\sum_{k=1}^{K_{h}}\pi_{k,h}\,N_{r_{h}}(m_{k,h},\bm{\Sigma}_{k,h}):m_{k,h}\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{h}},\,\bm{\Sigma}_{k,h}\in\mathbb{S}^{r_{h}}_{+},\,\pi_{k,h}\geq 0,\,\right.

(J.2)

\displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt\left.\sum_{k=1}^{K_{h}}\pi_{k,h}=1\;\mbox{for $k_{h}\in[K_{h}]$}\right\}.

(J.3)

By working with these prior classes,
estimating
\mu
and any
\nu_{h}
reduces to
estimating mixing proportions and component means and covariances in a multivariate Gaussian mixture.

If
r_{h}
for some
h\in[m]
is very large, we can model
\bm{\Sigma}_{k}
and
\bm{\Sigma}_{k,h}
by diagonal matrices.
In such situations, it might be essential to have more components in the Gaussian mixture to account for simplification in the covariance structure.

J.2
Nonparametric maximum likelihood estimators

An alternative approach for estimating the priors
\mu
and
\{\nu_{h}:h\in[m]\}
is to consider their nonparametric maximum likelihood estimators (NPMLEs).
This approach was advocated in
[
57
]
.
It is important to observe that, this estimator can suffer from the “curse of dimensionality” if
r+\widetilde{r}
, or any of the
r_{h}
, is large. However, if the signal strengths
(\bm{D}_{h})_{ii}
for
i\in[r_{h}]
and
h\in[m]
are sufficiently high, such “curse of dimensionality can be mitigated.
For constructing the NPMLEs, we approximate
\mathcal{P}
and all of the
\mathcal{P}_{\nu_{h}}
’s using discrete distributions with an appropriately chosen support.
In particular, we can set the support of
\mu
, denoted by
\mathcal{S}_{\mu}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{r+\widetilde{r}}
, as

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{\mu}

\displaystyle:=\left\{((\widehat{\bm{S}}^{L,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,1})^{-1}({\bm{U}}^{\mathrm{pc}}_{0,1})_{i*},\ldots,(\widehat{\bm{S}}^{L,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,m})^{-1}({\bm{U}}^{\mathrm{pc}}_{0,m})_{i*},\widehat{\bm{L}}^{-1}_{1}(\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{1})_{i*},\ldots,\right.

(J.4)

\displaystyle\hskip 250.00038pt\left.\widehat{\bm{L}}^{-1}_{\widetilde{m}}(\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{\widetilde{m}})_{i*}):i\in[N]\right\}.

(J.5)

In other words, each point in the support of
\mu
is one row in the
matrix

\begin{bmatrix}(\widehat{\bm{S}}^{L,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,1})^{-1}{\bm{U}}^{\mathrm{pc}}_{0,1}&\ldots&(\widehat{\bm{S}}^{L,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,m})^{-1}{\bm{U}}^{\mathrm{pc}}_{0,m}&\widehat{\bm{L}}^{-1}_{1}\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{1}&\ldots&\widehat{\bm{L}}^{-1}_{\widetilde{m}}\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{\widetilde{m}}\end{bmatrix}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times(r+\widetilde{r})}.

Then, we set

{\mathcal{P}}=\{\mbox{all prior $\mu$ supported on $\mathcal{S}_{\mu}$}\}.

(J.6)

Similarly, for each
h\in[m]
, the support of
\nu_{h}
, denoted by
\mathcal{S}_{\nu_{h}}\subset\mathbb{R}^{r_{h}}
, can be set at

\mathcal{S}_{\nu_{h}}:=\left\{(\widehat{\bm{S}}^{R,\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h})^{-1}({\bm{V}}^{\mathrm{pc}}_{0,h})_{i*}:i\in[p_{h}]\right\}.

Thus, each point in the support of
\nu_{h}
is a row in
(\widehat{\bm{S}}^{R}_{0,h})^{-1}{\bm{V}}_{0,h}
, and we then set

{\mathcal{P}}_{\nu_{h}}=\{\mbox{all prior $\nu_{h}$ supported on $\mathcal{S}_{\nu_{h}}$}\}.

(J.7)

By working with these discrete priors with a fixed support, estimating
\mu
and each
\nu_{h}
reduces to
estimating the mixing proportions in a mixture of degenerate Gaussians where the number of components equals the number of points in support. While these priors do not satisfy Assumption 5.1, yet because of the discrete structure the same theoretical conclusions can also be proven for them.

J.3
Effect of the numbers of retained principal components on atlas building in TEA-seq data

In order to study the impact of the choice of the number of principal components
r_{1}
and
r_{2}
used to approximate the signals in the high-dimensional modalities (ATAC and RNA) on the quality of the atlas constructed by Algorithm 1, we considered an experiment where we built the multimodal atlases following the technique outlined in Section 4 while varying the numbers of principal components in the high-dimensional modalities as follows: 10 ATAC PCs and 15 RNA PCs, 15 ATAC PCs and 20 RNA PCs, and 20 ATAC PCs and 20 RNA PCs.
We assessed the quality of the atlases in terms of the four clustering metrics, namely, Adjusted Rand Index, Average Silhouette Score, cLISI, and V-measure. We summarize our findings in Table
5
. The UMAP
visualization of the atlases constructed in the three settings is shown in Figure
7
.
Both the UMAP visualization and the clustering metrics suggest that the quality of the constructed atlas does not vary drastically with the choice of the number of principal components,
after the leading principal components explaining the maximum variability in the data are included.
The numbers of principal components can generally be determined by observing the scree plots of the empirical singular values of the data matrices and comparing the empirical spectrum
of the residual component (after subtracting out the low rank representation) with appropriately rescaled
Marcenko-Pastur distribution.

J.4
Effect of the number of retained principal components on atlas building in CITE-seq data

We conducted a similar experiment to study the impact of the choice of the number of principal components used to model the signal component in the RNA modality of the CITE-seq data described in Section
B
.
In that direction, we repeated the same atlas-building procedure described in Section
B
while varying the number of principal components used to model RNA in
\{45,50,55\}
.
The quantitative metrics are reported in Table
6
and the UMAP visualization of the three atlases in Figure
8
. We see that both the clustering metrics and the UMAP visualizations are comparable across the three choices of the number of retained principal components in the RNA modality, indicating that the relative robustness to the numbers of principal components used for atlas construction is consistent across data types and sequencing technologies.

Figure 8
:
UMAP representation of the embeddings constructed using OrchAMP in the CITE-seq data with different numbers of principal components for the RNA modality. Left: 45 RNA PCs, Middle: 50 RNA PCs, and Right: 55 RNA PCs
